# Roadmap, checkpoint 5, the hand-off (2026-10-24)

Owner: the roadmap owner of checkpoint 5. This file replaces the checkpoint 4 roadmap, whose result is kept, with the checkpoint 3 result, as a dated record at the end. It re-runs the plan from the areas' epic tables as the owners now hold them after folding the developer's 25 answers of 2026-10-24 and answering the claims of `RedTeam.md` 8.8, places every epic on two streams from 2026-11-02 with the scheduler in the Workflow folder, and checks every start against its dependencies by program. **Every date in this file and in its fragment is an estimate the developer is not bound by, never a commitment**; where a date could read as a promise the word "estimated" stands beside it.

Revision note, 2026-10-24, checkpoint 5: the developer's answers are in the plan. Decision 1 is yes (the one change that can break Saboteer's build inside the freeze, Modifiers' move onto the definition contract inside CL4, waits on a side branch and merges 2027-03-02, estimated); decision 2 is no (W4c, the Places gym, sits behind Saboteer's milestone and before any Corsairs launch); decision 3 is yes (the artist's window and hold test, the shipped agent context and the saves upgrade stay in front). Ships and moving decks (N6) join the pre-launch window after the Corsairs milestone. Saboteer's cut-over to the new UI and chat is scheduled first and lands 2027-01-13, the working day before the freeze, and the demo section is rewritten. The maintenance-branch story is dated to Saboteer's first Steam release, which the readers' round moved behind every Saboteer-tagged part and the first stable tags, GeoForge Pro leaves Saboteer's manifest in W10a's last story, the Unity 7 copy is a fourth story of U9, and the release scan is a second story of LV7d. The counts the owners and this file stated differently are reconciled to the owners' tables as they now stand. Round of independent readers: twelve findings landed on this file and are answered in the section "Red team: independent readers, checkpoint 5". The plan changed in six places: Saboteer's first Steam release now follows every Saboteer-tagged part, Corsairs' soak and the first stable tags, so it comes after the removals that cannot reach a maintenance branch; the stable-tag chain (Core's audit, Core 1.0, the tag gate and the release checklist) is dated; the gym (W4c) leaves Corsairs' class; Make playable is split (A7a in Saboteer's class, A7b the gym line); the layout description of Core's packing (R0d) is a gate before the freeze; the train that carries the console and the chat waits for both. The scheduler holds all of it and its output is the only source of every date below. Run it from the Pitch folder in bash: `python -I Revisited/Workflow/roadmap_svg.py <outdir>`.

## The result in six lines

- **884 dated gates in 119 epics**, 147.2 million tokens, 166.2 with the red-team rounds (15.4 million for 48 epics of eight stories or more, 3.6 million for four milestone rounds). Outside the date: 14 undated gates (1.9 million) and 21 backlog gates (3.3 million) priced and not scheduled. The checkpoint 4 plan had 847 gates, 140.9 and 159.9 million tokens.
- **Two milestones measured by the games, and one release.** Saboteer on the line, estimated **2028-01-06** (sprint 31, 534 gates finished; its soak R7b ends 2027-12-21); Corsairs Isle on the line, estimated **2028-04-19** (sprint 39, 668 gates); Saboteer's first Steam release, estimated **2028-06-06** (sprint 42), which waits for every Saboteer-tagged part, for Corsairs' soak and for the first stable tags, because from the day it ships Saboteer follows a maintenance branch that refuses removals; the plan ends, estimated, 2028-10-09 (sprint 51). With leave and public holidays priced in (0.93 gates per person per day) the estimates are 2028-04-05, 2028-08-14, 2028-10-12 and 2029-03-22. Checkpoint 4 said 2028-01-04, 2028-04-10 and 2028-09-08 for Saboteer, Corsairs and the end.
- **Capacity decides the dates.** The longest chain is 100 gates to Saboteer's soak, 111 to Corsairs' and 124 to the first release; with unlimited streams and no freeze Saboteer would be on the line 2027-04-13 and Corsairs 2027-04-29. Two streams at the decided rate carry 884 gates in about 442 working days each.
- **Saboteer's demo is due before 11 February 2027 and the plan meets it, with the new UI and chat in it and no days to spare.** The cut-over (42 gates in nine parts, with the Core, layout and Persistence work under it) is scheduled first and ends 2027-01-13; the freeze starts 2027-01-14. The window holds that work and almost nothing else, so the networked chat bridge (U4b), the minimal Send (A5a) and the demo's tag script (WF7a) fall after the festival, and Modifiers' move waits on its side branch until 2027-03-02 (estimated). It costs no days on either milestone.
- **Navigation, ships and moving decks included, runs after the Corsairs milestone and before any launch**: 51 gates in six epics (N2 13, N3 8, N4 10, N5 9, N6 5, N9 6) plus the spike's 5, estimated 2028-05-17 to 2028-08-28 (W9b, World's reachability check, ends 2028-09-19). The scheduler refuses any earlier start.
- **One question waits on an answer: when Saboteer ships (Decisions); the plan is built on the recommended answer.** Every decision the developer made is in the plan; the one thing the plan cannot do is hold a margin: the cut-over ends one working day before the freeze, and a loss of two days anywhere in the first ten weeks moves its last part behind the festival (the sensitivity table prints the date). The scheduler is `Workflow/roadmap_svg.py`; it checked 198 parts against their dependencies, the freeze, the Corsairs milestone gate, the gym's place behind Saboteer's milestone, the agents' context before every rewire after the cut-over, the paired merges and the first release after every Saboteer-tagged part, with no violations.

## How the plan is priced

The method is the checkpoint 4 method, unchanged except where marked. A story is one developer gate, and a gate is the calendar. An agent session reads, writes, tests and stops at the gate; a person reads the diff, runs what only a person can, decides and commits.

| Cost | Per unit | Assumption |
|---|---|---|
| Reading | 36 tokens per line in scope | 12 tokens per line, read three times across a story's life |
| Writing | 30 tokens per line written | 15 tokens of output per line, written twice (draft, fix after the gate) |
| Verify loops | 8,000 tokens each, 6 per story | recompile, console, test run, read back |
| Session overhead | 40,000 tokens per session, 2 per story | skills, instructions, memory, the status file |
| Fan-out | reading times 1.5 | applied to every epic that reads 8,000 lines or more |
| A deeper pitch | 1.28 million for 3 gates | 15,000 lines read, 3,000 written; the two pitches with a spike story are 4 gates and 1.41 million |
| A red-team round | 0.32 million per epic of 8 stories or more | four readers, measured in revision 4 |
| A milestone round | 0.91 million | eight readers over the whole, measured on the line |

So a story costs 128,000 tokens before its reading and writing. Lines read and written are the areas' own estimates (the line epics' are mine). Nothing here is measured except the red-team rounds; sprint 1 re-issues the rates. Tokens do not cover wall-clock: Unity compile and test loops and the gate's turnaround are clock.

**Calendar.** Sprints are 14 days from 2026-11-02. A working day is Monday to Friday. Off days: 2026-12-24, 25 and 28 to 31, 2027-01-01, 2027-12-24 and 27 to 31, 2028-12-25 to 29. **Reserve:** the last working day of every sprint with 8 or more working days is held back on both streams, 10 percent of capacity. **Leave:** none modelled other than the Christmas weeks; the sensitivity table prices 0.93 gates per person per day. The planning rate is the decided one: a gate per person per working day beside their own work.

**Streams.** Two, each a lineage of sessions reviewed by one person, one story per working day. A part is one item and runs on one stream; two parts of an epic may run on both. The scheduler is a list scheduler. What differs from checkpoint 4, all of it from the developer's answers of 2026-10-24:

- **The first tier.** The closure of the cut-over (UI4a, UI6b, CL3a, with L1r under them and the layout description R0d under L8a) is scheduled before everything else, longest chain inside that closure first. LM and LS, the manifest lines that carry Core's assemblies to the branches the games follow, wait for the console and the chat (UI4a, UI6b, CL3a) so the train arrives with them. Then the demo's files, then everything Saboteer on the line needs, then what Corsairs on the line adds, then the rest. A short list of items is boosted ahead of its class, as before; the boosts are priorities and never dependencies.
- **The freeze.** No Saboteer-tagged part runs from 2027-01-14 to 2027-03-01 (bug fixes and media only, build scenes frozen). Exceptions are manifest lines (LS, W10aL). A5a and WF1r are now tagged, because A5a puts its caller into Saboteer's repository. The four festival gates stay fixed days: 2027-02-03 (ship the demo build), 2027-02-09 (defects before the press preview), 2027-02-24 (festival fixes), 2027-03-02 (wrap up and re-test). Steam's partner page is checked before 14 December 2026; the freeze moves with whatever it says.
- **The gym.** W4c waits, by program, for every Saboteer-class part to end (answer 2). It is in no milestone's class: no Corsairs rewire needs it, and what waits on it (A7b, CL8, W9b) is after the Corsairs milestone.
- **The first release.** LV6m and WF5m wait, by program, for every Saboteer-tagged part, Corsairs' soak (R7c), Diagnostics and Studio.Online (R2b, L11b, L11c) and the first stable tags (CT, LV7c, WF5b), and the program refuses a Saboteer-tagged part that ends after they start.
- **Navigation's gate.** The first epic of Navigation after its spike waits, by program, for every Saboteer-class and Corsairs-class part to end.
- **No pin, no demo branch, no wait for Unity 6.7.** MV has a calendar date (not before 2027-01-04) and nothing depends on it; WF7b lists neither MV nor the U9 probe.
- **No preemption.** An item that has started finishes, so dates move by days when an input changes.
- **Paired merges.** The program refuses a gap of more than five working days between R6b and R6c, L8 and L8c, L1r and LM, L1r and LS, A5a and WF1r, and LV6m and WF5m, because each pair merges to a followed branch together or follows the other directly.
- **The agents' context before a rewire.** CL5 and LV4a end before L3, L4 and every other Saboteer rewire. The cut-over parts (UI1 to UI6b, CL3a, WF7a, A5a, WF1r, UI4b) are the one exception, because answer 23 puts them in front of the freeze and the context cannot be there first; the program checks every other part.

**What stays human.** Playing a gym and judging feel (L4, R3), judging a look (UI5, W6, W7, IN1, L12), authoring a place as the designer (W4), the artist's import and Send (A1, A5), opening each project on a new editor (MV, LS, LM, LV5), capturing Saboteer's frame figures on the reference PC (R7a, R7b), a two-process test watched by a person (NW2), the Corsairs F2 capture (W2b), approving the Persistence pitch in November (PP), choosing the demo tag and running the Steam deploy, closing a spike's go or no-go in writing, and every commit.

## How the epics were assigned to the two milestones

A seed is an epic whose area file says it rewires, deletes code from or moves a game (Game column: Saboteer, or both, for the first milestone; Corsairs for the second), except those the area says neither game needs. Everything a seed depends on joins its class. The method is the checkpoint 4 method; three things are new. The cut-over closure is the first tier (above). W4c is in no class, with a program-checked place behind Saboteer's milestone. The first release (LV6m, WF5m) is the one item that waits for everything Saboteer-tagged.

- **Cut-over and demo seeds:** CL3a, UI1, UI2, UI3, UI4a1, UI4a2, UI4a, UI6a, UI6b, WF7a. Their closure is the first class.
- **Saboteer's seeds:** L1, LS, L8c, L11a1, L12, CL1b, CL2, R1b, R6d, R7b, UI1b, UI4b, UI5, W2a, W4a, W4b, W10a, W10aL, NW2g, NW2, NW4a, NW4b, NW10, A4, A5a, LV3a, LV5a, LV5s, LV5b1, LV5b2, LV5b3, LV5c0, LV7a, LV7d, WF5a, A7a.
- **Corsairs' seeds:** LM, L8c, L11a1, L11a2, L11d2, CL3b, R6c, R7c, W1b, W2b, W2c, W3, W5, W7, W8, W9a, W10b, NW3b, UN3, UN4a, UN4b, UN5, UN6, WF7b.
- **Neither (after Corsairs, before any launch):** the gym (W4c), ArtPipeline beyond A5a and A7a (A5b, A6, A7b), Diagnostics (R2) and Studio.Online (L11b), the strangers that need Corsairs' packages (LV5c, LV5d) and the Core-only compile without Entities (LV5m), Navigation (NV2 to NV9, N6 included) and W9b, Living's window pages, the first-release chain (Core's audit and Core 1.0, the tag gate and the release checklist, the candidate tag, LV6m and WF5m, dated this run), Workflow's release train beyond the first beta, selling, Persistence's durable trade, the editor face (CL7, UI7b), the scenario proof (CL8), the agent operations and the Unity 6.7 move.
- **Tags.** Saboteer-tagged parts (they edit Saboteer's code, scenes or repository, so the freeze keeps them out of 14 January to 1 March): A4, A5a, CL3a, L11a1, L12, L3, L4, L7a, L8, L8c, LS, MV, NW2, NW4b, R1b, R2a, R3a, R4, R6b, R7b, UI1, UI2, UI3, UI4a, UI4b, UI5, UI6a, UI6b, UN6, W10a, W10aL, W4a, W4b, WF1r, WF7a, WF7b. Exempt: LS and W10aL (manifest lines).

**Where an owner's class differs from the plan's.** The owners file classes as proposals; the class here follows the seed rule. The differences are below, none of them a count.

| Epic | Owner's class | Plan's class | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| CL4 | Corsairs | Saboteer, by closure | LV2, UI7a, W1a and W4a wait on it, and Modifiers' adoption is on a side branch, not in a milestone |
| CL6, CL7 | Corsairs | after Corsairs, before launch | an operation per package is not what Corsairs on the line needs; the method stays as at checkpoint 4 |
| R2 | Corsairs | after Corsairs, before launch | nothing in Corsairs on the line waits on Diagnostics; L11b, CL6d and LV5c follow it |
| UN1, UN2, UN7, UN8, UN9 | Saboteer (U1, U2), Corsairs (U7, U8), planning (U9) | after Corsairs, before launch | no game rewire; nothing waits for 6.7 or for the probe (decided), so MV and UN9 are boosted in time and not in class |
| W2b, W2c | Saboteer (the epic) | Corsairs | W10a needs only W2a; W2b is Corsairs' own rewire and W2c waits for W10a's last story |
| NW10 | Corsairs | Saboteer | L3's driver story waits for its probe, and L3 is Saboteer's first rewire |
| UI4b | Corsairs | Saboteer | the UI owner's rule: if N4 is Saboteer's, U4b joins it; N4a is |
| WF2, WF1a, WF1b | Saboteer | demo files | A5a, UI4a1 and WF7a need them first |
| WF7a | Saboteer, before the freeze | demo files, but after the festival | the window is full (below) |
| W4c | Corsairs (the plan of checkpoint 5) | after Corsairs, before launch | the World owner's class is "behind Saboteer's milestone and before any Corsairs launch"; no Corsairs rewire waits on the gym, so it is no seed (reader finding 12) |
| A7 | after Corsairs, before launch | A7a Saboteer, A7b after Corsairs | only the gym line waits for W4c (reader finding 5) |
| LV6m, WF5m | Saboteer (checkpoint 5 draft) | after both | a shipped game follows a branch that refuses removals, so the release waits for them (reader findings 1, 6, 7) |

## The critical path and what runs beside it

**The spine to Saboteer** is 100 gates: L1 > CL1 > CL4 > UI7a > LV2 > LV4a > L3 > W4a > W4b > L11d1 > W10a > R7b. **The spine to Corsairs** is 111 gates, through Saboteer's soak and UN6 to Corsairs' soak. **The spine to Saboteer's first Steam release** is 124 gates: it continues from Corsairs' soak through Navigation's first epic (NV2a), the racing and underwater strangers (LV5c, LV5d) and the release checklist (LV7c) to LV6m, because Living's checklist waits for the matrix; without that wait the release would be 2028-05-19. Navigation's longest chain, behind the Corsairs milestone, is 138 gates to NV9 and 140 to W9b; the gym (W4c) runs after Saboteer's soak and nothing in the Corsairs chain waits for it (A7b and CL8 do). With no limit on streams and no freeze, Saboteer would land 2027-04-13 and Corsairs 2027-04-29 and the plan would end 2028-01-04, held there by the calendar date of the floor move; two streams at the decided rate take 884 gates in about 442 working days each, so **capacity decides the end**.

Beside the spine: the cut-over closure fills both streams until 2027-01-13; CL2 and CL5 (the pipeline bridge and the shipped agent context) come right after it, then the contract (CL4) on its side branch inside the freeze, the vocabularies (R0, R1a), the runner (LV2) and the baseline soak (R7a); Navigation's spike NV1 runs 2027-05-03 to 2027-05-07, before GeoForge's run-list read is built (W2a 2027-05-19 to 2027-06-08).

Each milestone is followed by an eight-lens red-team round (0.91 million tokens) and its fix gates (FX), and this file is re-issued with the measured gate rate and token rate.

## Saboteer's demo and Next Fest

**Yes, the demo is due before 11 February 2027, and under this plan it is met, with the new UI and chat in it and no margin.** Steam Next Fest is 22 February to 1 March 2027, the press preview is 11 February, registration is about 10 January; the dates come from third-party sites, and the developer checks Steam's own partner page before 14 December 2026 (a calendar item). The freeze moves with whatever it says. Every date below is an estimate.

- **What the demo contains.** Today's Saboteer plus the cut-over (answer 23): the new console over the operation list (UI3, to 2027-01-05), Saboteer's ten commands on it (CL3a, 2027-01-08 to 2027-01-11), the new chat window, its settings page and Saboteer's cut-over to Switchboard with the local transport and its thin ChatNetwork (UI4a1, UI4a2, UI4a, last gate 2027-01-12), the widgets and the split UI package (UI1, UI2), input contexts and the layout section (UI6a, UI6b, 2027-01-11 to 2027-01-12), and the packages under them. A tester who saved a HUD layout in an earlier build sees the default HUD once; the demo is exempt from the saves rule. 114 gates are finished by the assumed build day, 2027-02-04.
- **What waits.** The networked chat bridge (U4b, 2027-07-05 to 2027-07-08, which retires the chat corruption above U+00FF), the minimal Send and the artist ruleset (A5a 2027-03-12 to 2027-03-17, WF1r 2027-03-18) and the demo's tag script and template files (WF7a 2027-03-29 to 2027-03-31). The window holds the cut-over closure and almost nothing else: both streams are busy from 2026-11-02 to 2027-01-13 with 83 gates of the first tier. The demo is tagged by hand.
- **The side branch.** Modifiers' move onto the definition contract (inside CL4, 2027-02-10 to 2027-02-24) is built on `definition-contract` during the freeze, with `beta` merged into the branch at the end of each sprint, and merges 2027-03-02, estimated. It is the one change that could break Saboteer's build, and it costs no days: nothing waits for Modifiers' adoption.
- **The exposure.** Nothing is pinned, so the packages Saboteer follows keep changing under the demo: Core on `#dev`, GeoForge on `#dev_1.0` and nine packages on their default branch. Before the build, the plan changes Core (L1 2026-11-02 to 2026-11-10, L1r, L2c, CL1, CL2 2027-01-14 to 2027-01-21, CL5 2027-01-25 to 2027-02-02), Inventory and Decals (LV1b 2026-11-04 to 2026-11-09), the UI package and Chat (UI1 to UI4a) and Persistence's package id (L8a, a new slice, no consumer). L1 moves Core's console and object folders into assemblies of their own, which GeoForge, Chat and VolumetricFog name, so L1r and the manifest edits (LM and LS, both 2027-01-13) wait for the console and the chat (UI4a, UI6b, CL3a): Core's assemblies, the references, the console and the chat reach the branches Saboteer follows as one train on 2027-01-13, the working day before the freeze, and not when each part ends. Parts that run inside the window and are not Saboteer-tagged: CL2 2027-01-14 to 2027-01-21, NW10 2027-01-14, NW2g 2027-01-15, L11t 2027-01-18 to 2027-01-19, UN9 2027-01-20 to 2027-01-26, CL5 2027-01-25 to 2027-02-02, WF1a 2027-01-27 to 2027-01-28, WF1b 2027-01-29 to 2027-02-16, WF2b 2027-02-03 to 2027-02-09, CL4 2027-02-10 to 2027-02-24, UN1a 2027-02-17 to 2027-02-23, R0 2027-02-25 to 2027-03-11, UI7a 2027-02-25 to 2027-03-02. All are additive to the branches or on the side branch.
- **The margin.** The cut-over's last part ends 2027-01-13, the working day before the freeze. At 0.93 gates per person per day it ends 2027-03-29, after the festival; with one person away in sprints 3 and 4 it ends 2027-03-16. The order inside the window puts the console first (UI3 2027-01-05) and the chat cut-over last, so a slip of a few days costs the chat cut-over (UI4a, with UI6b and CL3a) and keeps the new console in the demo.
- **One rule is relaxed for the cut-over.** The shipped agent context (CL5, 2027-01-25 to 2027-02-02; LV4a 2027-03-18 to 2027-03-23) cannot come first: the cut-over's sessions run on the studio's own skill files. Every later rewire waits for both, and the first is L3 (2027-04-15 to 2027-04-29).
- **What the freeze costs.** No days on either milestone: the same milestones come out with the cut-over scheduled first or not, with the freeze from 14 January or from the build date, or with no freeze (the sensitivity table). What it takes is the order: the first rewire L3 starts 2027-04-15 (was 2027-03-15), and CL2, CL5, UN9, PWO, R0, A5a and WF7a start 2 to 14 weeks later than at checkpoint 4.
- **The festival gates.** Ship the demo build on 2027-02-03, defects on 2027-02-09, festival fixes on 2027-02-24, wrap up and re-test on 2027-03-02. A fix to a package Saboteer uses is made on that package's branch, and nothing is pinned.
- **Not met, and not meant to be.** The line cannot be in the demo: 114 gates are finished by the build date and Saboteer on the line needs 534. Steam's own steps (store page review, demo app, build review, registration) are in no file I read and are not in the gate count.

One row per epic, grouped by the first class that needs it (Demo is the cut-over and the demo's files). ## What each epic delivers

One paragraph per epic: what it delivers, what it depends on, whether it delivers value alone, why it is in its class, and the human-only steps. Dates are estimated start and end.


### Line and Core

**L1 Core as assemblies.** Core drawn as assemblies inside its repo: the engine-only assembly carries no Unity package, the world-object and console folders sit in assemblies of their own awaiting their homes, the folder cycles are broken, Core's packing is checked for engine types, and the clean rebuild is re-measured against Audit section 6 (in Corsairs no Spellbound assembly starts compiling until Entities Graphics finishes, 27.9 s of a 35.4 s wall). One story moves Core's repo to the settled layout. Three more, L1r (reader finding 4), add the new assembly references to GeoForge, Chat, VolumetricFog and Modifiers, which name Core's console or object namespaces in seven files I checked, and to Corsairs' assemblies (WorldForge, WorldSystem, TalentSystem and the tests), where 68 files use those namespaces and WorldForge's reference only Core's main assembly. L1 merges to the branch the games follow only together with L1r, LS and LM, and LS and LM wait for the console and the chat, so the train arrives as one. Nothing is pinned, so nothing gates the start. Its acceptance test is the CoreOnly manifest compiled by hand in a clean project; LV5a later runs it in the harness. L1 and L1r stand in the first tier of the plan with the cut-over, because UI1 stands on Core's assemblies and L1 merges only with L1r. Depends on nothing. Value alone: Yes: the compiler enforces Core's boundary. The compile gain for a project without Entities arrives when the Entities package leaves Core's manifest, in UN4b's last story; this epic measures the baseline. 10 gates, 2.20 million tokens, estimated 2026-11-02 to 2027-01-08. Needed for: L1 and L1r are the cut-over before the freeze, because UI1, L2c and CL1 wait on L1 and L1 merges only with L1r. Parts: L1, 7 gates, 2026-11-02 to 2026-11-10, after nothing; L1r, 3 gates, 2027-01-06 to 2027-01-08, after L1.

**LM Corsairs follows the layout move.** L1 moves Core's repo and LV1b moves Inventory and Decals to the settled layout, so Corsairs' manifest lines for those three, which follow branches, need the ?path= form. One story edits them in Corsairs in the same sitting as the restructure merge (Living's claim, accepted). Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), L1r (Core as assemblies), LV1b (Layout and probes), UI4a (Switchboard), UI6b (Input contexts, layout as a Persist...), CL3a (Retire the old console, convert Sab...), so Core's assemblies, the references, the console and the chat reach the followed branches together. Value alone: Yes: Corsairs resolves its packages. 1 gate, 0.14 million tokens, estimated 2027-01-13 to 2027-01-13. Needed for: Corsairs Isle on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Corsairs. Human-only: Open Corsairs once.

**LS Saboteer follows the layout move.** The same story for Saboteer's manifest lines, which follow Core on its dev branch and Inventory and Decals on their default branches. It edits a manifest line and no scene or code, so it is allowed inside the code freeze. It replaces the pin gate of the last plan and the fifth story of MV. Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), L1r (Core as assemblies), LV1b (Layout and probes), UI4a (Switchboard), UI6b (Input contexts, layout as a Persist...), CL3a (Retire the old console, convert Sab...), so Core's assemblies, the references, the console and the chat reach the followed branches together. Value alone: Yes: Saboteer resolves its packages. 1 gate, 0.14 million tokens, estimated 2027-01-13 to 2027-01-13. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Saboteer. Human-only: Open Saboteer once.

**PK Packing slice.** Core's pure wire half that the developer already has in progress, its own gate so Networking does not wait on the rest of Core: pack into a caller-owned or pooled buffer, decode limits, the bit writer with varints and quantisation, standard reads as fast as the Bitwise path, the pure delta pack and unpack. No old reader is kept; a changed layout means re-save. Depends on nothing. Value alone: Yes: the measured packing gaps 1 to 10 close. 5 gates, 0.79 million tokens, estimated 2027-05-10 to 2027-05-17. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because NW1 waits on it.

**L2c Core's ground vocabulary, section attribute and reference frame noun.** What the line puts in Core that no area epic carries: the section attribute without versions or migration steps, the ground vocabulary (surface sample and occupancy source), and the reference frame noun (ReferenceFrameId, IReferenceFrame, ReferenceFramePose, the per-world ReferenceFrameDirectory and ReferenceFrameChecks.RequireConstantUp). The noun moved here from R3a because World's surface request, WorldForge's guard, Networking's partition case and the Places stamp compile against it months before R3a (reader finding 6). The redirect table, section versions and migration steps are gone with the compatibility stories. The rest of the line's L2 is CL1, R0, R1a and CL4, counted there once. Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies). Value alone: Partly: each declaration is usable the day it lands. 3 gates, 0.48 million tokens, estimated 2026-11-17 to 2026-11-19. Needed for: the cut-over and the demo before the freeze, because PP, L8a, UI4a1 wait on it.

**L3 WorldObjects package.** WorldObjects becomes a package: the folders leave Core, a world instance that steps its own physics scene (decided 2026-10-24), a replaceable presenter under the server define, change and resolve named and enforced, stable ids through the allocation seam, no statics; Modifiers' stat binding and WorldForge's twelve files are rewired and both games follow. One story is the collision part that compiles in every build (Runtime's claim, confirmed by the pitch). The spatial index and the awakening job are UN4's stories. It waits for N10's probe (NW10), whose written rule says who steps a world that predicts, before the driver story is frozen. Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), R0 (Simulation vocabulary in Core), CL1 (Operations in Core), R1a (Observation declarations and per-wo...), WF2a (Templates), W1g (The world model), R7a (Baseline soak), PWO (WorldObjects pitch with its spike), CL5 (AI context), LV4a (Context and the first hour), NW10 (Stepping ownership when a world pre...). Value alone: Yes: the folders leave Core and become a package; Core is Entities-free only when UN4b ends. 10 gates, 1.92 million tokens, estimated 2027-04-15 to 2027-04-29. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because W4a, L6, LV5b2 wait on it. Human-only: Play both games after the rewire.

**L4 StateMachine and Character.** Controller becomes StateMachine and Character (Body and Rig assemblies, the rig contract asset); the five unused dependencies go; the ragdoll builder and both games' copied state bindings come home. Walk, run, jump and fall are a Character sample over StateMachine; the house set of states lives in Studio.Gameplay. One of the ten stories is the restructure of the Controller repo (Living's claim, accepted). Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), WF2a (Templates), R7a (Baseline soak), CL5 (AI context), LV4a (Context and the first hour). Value alone: Yes. 10 gates, 1.55 million tokens, estimated 2027-06-09 to 2027-06-23. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because R5a, R4p, A2 wait on it. Human-only: Play the two gyms and judge feel.

**L6 Inventory and Modifiers audit.** An audit epic for Inventory and Modifiers: conform both to the bar, measure them against the games, red-team their public surface, and confirm the seams that Studio.Gameplay and R6 build on. Sized by me, since no area priced it; one of the five is Modifiers' restructure (Living's claim, accepted). Depends on L3 (WorldObjects package), CL4 (Authoring contract and check seam), UI1 (UI split, truthful package, UIKit,...). Value alone: Yes. 5 gates, 0.78 million tokens, estimated 2027-08-12 to 2027-08-18. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because PG, R6a, L11a1 wait on it.

**L7a GeoForge presenter seam.** GeoForge gets a replaceable chunk presenter under the server define (the internal prefab field opens), the surface case query seam, and the demo controller and legacy jobs go; two stories move GeoForge and WorldForge to the settled layout. The rest of the line's L7 is W1, W2, W4b, W7 and UN6, counted there. Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), CL1 (Operations in Core), LV1b (Layout and probes), CL5 (AI context), LV4a (Context and the first hour). Value alone: Yes. 7 gates, 1.16 million tokens, estimated 2027-04-21 to 2027-04-29. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because W2a, NW2, W4b wait on it.

**L8 Persistence rewrite.** Persistence rewritten under the studio id over Core's section attribute: a storage seam, owners' sections, unknown ids reported, a section header with name, layout fingerprint and length (decided 2026-10-24). Durations are seconds. One of the six stories is the restructure of the Persistence repo (Living's claim, accepted). Designed after the Persistence pitch, which also designs the upgrade of a shipped game's old saves; the upgrade is built in L8u and both games' store files are converted in L8c. L8 merges to the branch the games follow only together with L8c. L8a, 2 gates, is the first slice (write and read one section with Core's header), which UI's U4a and U6b need before the freeze; the other four gates follow after the festival. Depends on PP (Persistence pitch with its spike), L2c (Core's ground vocabulary, section a...), LV1b (Layout and probes), R0d (the layout description, first in the closure). Value alone: Yes, for every consumer that saves. 6 gates, 0.89 million tokens, estimated 2026-12-08 to 2027-08-26. Needed for: the cut-over and the demo before the freeze (and later classes for its other parts), because UI4a1, UI6b wait on it. Parts: L8a, 2 gates, 2026-12-08 to 2026-12-09, after PP, L2c, LV1b, R0d; L8, 4 gates, 2027-08-23 to 2027-08-26, after L8a.

**L8c Persistence: both games' stores converted.** Saboteer's settings, progress and chat settings files and Corsairs' stores (characters, inventory, equipment, stats, UI layout, GeoForge edits) move from the old static entry point and the com.spellbound package id onto the rewritten Persistence under the studio id. A placeholder sized by the Persistence pitch. L8 changes the package id, removes the static entry point and keeps no reader of the old layout, and both manifests name the old package with no branch, so L8 merges to the followed branch only together with L8c (reader finding 7). Depends on L8 (Persistence rewrite). Value alone: No: it is the games' own conversion; without it both games lose saving the day L8 merges. 4 gates, 0.64 million tokens, estimated 2027-08-27 to 2027-09-01. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Saboteer, and R7b waits on it. Human-only: Save and load once in each game.

**L8u Persistence: upgrade of a shipped game's old saves.** The upgrade the developer decided on 2026-10-24: in a shipped game the upgrade of its players' old saves runs on its own, and a customer may extend Persistence for their own players however they want. Detection stays (a section header with name, layout fingerprint and length; an old layout is refused by name); this is the part that then carries the player's save across. A placeholder sized by the Persistence pitch, which designs it. It is built before the first ship to a customer, and a Saboteer Steam release built on the line is that ship (reader finding 9); LV7d, the stored-format release gate, waits with it, and R7b waits for both. The Next Fest demo is exempt. Depends on PP (Persistence pitch with its spike), L8 (Persistence rewrite). Value alone: Yes, for every shipped game that saves. 4 gates, 0.65 million tokens, estimated 2027-11-29 to 2027-12-02. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because R7b waits on it. Human-only: Open a save written by today's Saboteer in a build made from the line.

**L8b Persistence: durable trade, per-entity records, database seam.** A placeholder sized by the Persistence pitch: one durable unit across two containers' sections (a gift between two characters is both written or neither), per-entity records loaded on login, partial region loads, and a database implementation of the storage seam. It serves the MMO and any game with accounts, so neither game waits on it. Depends on PP (Persistence pitch with its spike), L8 (Persistence rewrite), R6a (Inventory transactions), NW2 (PurrNet adapter and first consumers). Value alone: Yes, for the MMO server and any game with accounts. 6 gates, 0.89 million tokens, estimated 2028-08-03 to 2028-08-11. Needed for: neither milestone; it follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch.

**L12 Decals, Volumetrics, TetherShop to the bar.** Volumetrics renamed from VolumetricFog, Decals' URP default as a bridge under the server define, TetherShop in its own repo with its game-named kinds moved to its sample, every manifest a floor at 6000.3, every bridge with a range. One of the eight stories is VolumetricFog's restructure (Living's claim, accepted). Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), WF2a (Templates), LV1b (Layout and probes). Value alone: Yes. 8 gates, 1.15 million tokens, estimated 2027-10-27 to 2027-11-08. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Saboteer, and R7b waits on it. Human-only: Judge the fog and rope look after the rename.

**MV Move to Unity 6.7 LTS.** Every project and dev project moves to Unity 6.7 LTS when it ships (expected at the end of 2026; the studio edits on 6.4 until then), using the Move story template from UN1. It waits for UN8's canary so what breaks is known first. No epic waits for it (decided 2026-10-24): if 6.7 is late, L3 and L4 are written on 6.4 and the move carries them. Seven stories (reader finding 10): the four of the move itself, and three the 6.7 summary names (`UnityChanges.md`): Unity's Localization migration in Saboteer's eight files, since the package moves to 2.0.0 in the first 6.7 beta; the saved-reference cycle rules that become compile errors (11 files in Modifiers, 3 in Core, 1 in Corsairs); and the SSAO settings copied by hand into a Volume in both games together with the C++ runtime files in the Steam upload. The fifth story of checkpoint 3, which took Saboteer off the demo pins, is gone with the pins. The floor compile and the 6.3 test row do not wait for it. Saboteer's milestone no longer includes the move. Depends on UN1a (The Unity ledger), UN8 (CoreCLR and domain-reload readiness). Value alone: Yes. 7 gates, 0.98 million tokens, estimated 2027-03-29 to 2027-04-07. Needed for: neither milestone; it follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch. Human-only: Open each project on the new editor.

**FL Floor moves to 6000.7 when 6.3 support ends.** The floor moves to 6000.7 when 6.3 LTS support ends in December 2027: the 6.3 test row is dropped, the floor compile moves up, every manifest's floor is raised. The first Unity cycle's floor-move story. Depends on LV7a (Gates), MV (Move to Unity 6.7 LTS). Value alone: Yes. 2 gates, 0.29 million tokens, estimated 2027-12-13 to 2027-12-14. Needed for: neither milestone; it follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch.

**IN1 Grass and impostors.** Instancing's mass drawing from non-interactive placements and the scrolling cover grid, culling and impostors, with the grass measurement first. A placeholder priced from revision 4's Masses epic until the Instancing pitch replaces it. Depends on PIN (Instancing internals pitch), UN7 (Batch renderer and mesh LOD). Value alone: Yes. 4 gates, 0.57 million tokens, estimated 2028-08-29 to 2028-09-04. Needed for: neither milestone; it follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch. Human-only: Judge the grass.


### Command line

**CL1 Operations in Core.** The operation attributes, catalog (marked assemblies only), tokenizer and parser, host, run, result, caller, the built-in help, about, history, stats and observation operations, the effect gate and audience check, and tests. Its first story is the reflection check: operation discovery under build stripping on Mono and IL2CPP against Unity's notes for 6.7 and the next major, with a go or no-go; a no-go moves discovery to a source generator. Its second checks that a Play Mode test can wait on a custom yield instruction. The roadmap splits it in two. CL1 is stories 1 to 10, the host, catalog, audience tag and result type that UI's console needs before the freeze. CL1b is stories 11 to 13: state replay of the lines half, the target marker on an operation and the router seam (`IOperationRouter`, `OperationHost.Router`, `RunHere`), which only Networking's operation stories read. Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies). Value alone: Yes: it replaces the three console mechanisms with one that has tests. 13 gates, 2.26 million tokens, estimated 2026-11-20 to 2027-10-28. Needed for: CL1 is the cut-over before the freeze, because UI3 and CL3a wait on it; CL1b is Saboteer on the line, because Networking's operation stories wait on it. Parts: CL1, 10 gates, 2026-11-20 to 2026-12-04, after L1; CL1b, 3 gates, 2027-10-26 to 2027-10-28, after CL1.

**CL2 Pipeline bridge and spellbound_run.** The Pipeline bridge assembly and the `spellbound_run` command, the caller policy and settings, a development build proven with the runtime flag, a fixture project driven by the Unity CLI in a test, and Volumetrics' three commands converted as the first package on the registry. It also runs on the 6000.3 floor row. Depends on CL1 (Operations in Core). Value alone: Yes: an agent can run every Core and game operation from the Unity CLI. 6 gates, 0.94 million tokens, estimated 2027-01-14 to 2027-01-21. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Saboteer, and CL5 waits on it.

**CL3 Retire the old console, convert Saboteer and Corsairs.** The three old attributes, the command interface, the two registries, the router and the console logger leave Core; Saboteer's ten command classes and Corsairs' fifty-seven methods convert; Chat's sample and registry are deleted. The roadmap splits it by game (a claim to the command line area, since the owner files one epic): CL3a, 2 gates, converts Saboteer's ten classes (539 lines) once the new console view exists, and sits in front of the freeze so the demo shows the new console; CL3b, 4 gates, converts Corsairs' commands (2,332 lines), removes the old types and waits for WorldObjects' preset and spawn operations (R8a), because only Corsairs' commands call them. UI3 deletes the views; CL3b deletes the registries and the logger. Depends on CL1 (Operations in Core), UI3 (ConsolePanel and ConsoleView over t...), L3 (WorldObjects package), UI4a (Switchboard), R8a (WorldObjects operations, object lay...). Value alone: Yes once the new view exists: it deletes debt. 6 gates, 1.34 million tokens, estimated 2027-01-08 to 2028-04-19. Needed for: CL3a is the cut-over before the freeze, because the demo shows the new console with Saboteer's commands on it; CL3b is Corsairs Isle on the line. Parts: CL3a, 2 gates, 2027-01-08 to 2027-01-11, after CL1, UI3; CL3b, 4 gates, 2028-04-13 to 2028-04-19, after CL3a, L3, UI4a, R8a.

**CL4 Authoring contract and check seam.** The authored-definition contract, the report and runner with the inspector banner and previewer, the check seam in Core.Editor as one interface, `IDefinitionCheck<T>`, and one attribute (a package adds its own check to a definition another package owns, and Core never changes for a new package), the draft folder and approve (an agent may not approve its own drafts unless one setting grants it), validate and preview operations, and a package folder as a subject. Story 10, the check seam, opens with the proof that `TypeCache` finds an attributed class implementing a closed generic interface, with `MarkedAssemblyScan` as the fallback. Modifiers adopts the contract as the first probe on a branch called `definition-contract`, built during the freeze and merged on 2 March 2027 (estimated); it is the one change that could break Saboteer's build inside the freeze, and it waits on that side branch (decided 2026-10-24). Stories 11 to 13 (the authored-key scan with the stored value text, `core revise`, the banner path) are CL4b. No format version, no migration step and no written note of a lost value: a changed format means re-author. Depends on CL1 (Operations in Core). Value alone: Partly: valuable for Modifiers alone; the agent flow needs CL2. 13 gates, 2.08 million tokens, estimated 2027-02-10 to 2027-10-08. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because UI7a, LV2, W1a wait on it. Human-only: A person approves drafts in the window. Parts: CL4, 10 gates, 2027-02-10 to 2027-02-24, after CL1; CL4b, 3 gates, 2027-10-06 to 2027-10-08, after CL4.

**CL5 AI context.** The six agent-context headings, the generated operation blocks hash-checked at release, core context, core context-install, core about and core ledger, with Volumetrics' file as the pilot. Seven stories: the attack round added one. The dangling-identifier check is Living's (LV4a) and runs right after, so a package's hand-written headings are verified before the first rewire. Both are boosted: the agents that do the rewires learn each package's front door, rules and operations from this context and not from its source (reader finding 1). Depends on CL1 (Operations in Core), CL2 (Pipeline bridge and spellbound_run). Value alone: Yes. 7 gates, 1.05 million tokens, estimated 2027-01-25 to 2027-02-02. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because LV4a, L7a, L3 wait on it.

**CL6 Every package's operations.** One story per package, each a read-state operation with tests, landing with that package's first epic: twenty stories in six parts (CL6a Persistence, Character and the console; CL6b the three small packages; CL6c Inventory with the world seams; CL6d Networking and Diagnostics; CL6e Places, Horizon and ArtPipeline; CL6n Navigation, which waits for the ground field). WorldObjects' operations are R8's, not these. The artpipeline operations gain a --keep gate. Depends on CL2 (Pipeline bridge and spellbound_run), L8 (Persistence rewrite), L4 (StateMachine and Character), UI3 (ConsolePanel and ConsoleView over t...), UI4a (Switchboard), L12 (Decals, Volumetrics, TetherShop to...), L6 (Inventory and Modifiers audit), L7a (GeoForge presenter seam), W1a (The world model), UN5 (Instancing seam), NW2 (PurrNet adapter and first consumers), R2a (Diagnostics), W4a (Places core), W6a (Horizon draws), A1a (ArtPipeline core), NV2a (The ground field). Value alone: Each alone, with its package. 20 gates, 4.37 million tokens, estimated 2028-07-04 to 2028-09-22. Needed for: neither milestone; it follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch. Parts: CL6a, 6 gates, 2028-07-04 to 2028-07-12, after CL2, L8, L4, UI3, UI4a; CL6b, 3 gates, 2028-09-06 to 2028-09-08, after CL2, L12; CL6c, 5 gates, 2028-08-22 to 2028-08-28, after CL2, L6, L7a, W1a, UN5; CL6d, 2 gates, 2028-09-20 to 2028-09-21, after CL2, NW2, R2a; CL6e, 3 gates, 2028-08-01 to 2028-08-03, after CL2, W4a, W6a, A1a; CL6n, 1 gate, 2028-09-22 to 2028-09-22, after CL2, NV2a.

**CL7 Editor face.** The Operations tab and the Drafts tab content, the completion data the console draws from, and the form generator, inside the Spellbound window. Depends on CL1 (Operations in Core), CL4 (Authoring contract and check seam), CL4b (Authoring contract and check seam), LV2 (Runner and read tier), UI3 (ConsolePanel and ConsoleView over t...). Value alone: Yes. 4 gates, 0.60 million tokens, estimated 2028-07-06 to 2028-07-12. Needed for: neither milestone; it follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch.

**CL8 Scenario on a stranger project in CI.** The cave-with-ore scenario scripted with the Unity CLI against a stranger project of five packages, run in CI. Steps 7, 12 and 15 (the Navigation lines) are skipped until Navigation's N2 and N5 land, so the scenario does not wait for Navigation. Depends on CL2 (Pipeline bridge and spellbound_run), CL4 (Authoring contract and check seam), CL5 (AI context), LV5a (StrangersDev), CL6a (Every package's operations), CL6e (Every package's operations), W4c (Places gym and checks), R8a (WorldObjects operations, object lay...). Value alone: No: it is the acceptance test. 3 gates, 0.48 million tokens, estimated 2028-09-11 to 2028-09-13. Needed for: neither milestone; it follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch.


### Runtime

**R0 Simulation vocabulary in Core.** Core's 64-bit tick and tick rate, the tick source seam with its fixed-step default and the never-backwards test, the up field, the seeded stream with golden vectors and a battery run, the instance id with its allocation seam, the determinism harness in the testing assembly, and the seconds round trip: a save holds no tick rate, durations are written in seconds and converted at load, and nothing is refused for a rate (decided). Ten stories: the attack round added the float-mode measurement, the claims round the tick source seam and the fold of checkpoint 5 the clock in Core with its driver, started with one call in a scene that has no world instance. The tick rate and the fixed step are settings read once, 20 and 50 as the starting values, and a test shows that changing the rate changes no code. Split by the roadmap: R0d (1 gate, the layout description of Core's packing, one function that the section header of the first Persistence slice and the surface writer both call) is first in the cut-over closure, and L8a waits for it; R0 is the other nine gates. Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), L2c (Core's ground vocabulary, section a...). Value alone: Yes: a package can drop Time.time and UnityEngine.Random. 10 gates, 1.38 million tokens, estimated 2026-12-07 to 2027-03-11. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because PWO, L3, W1a wait on it (R0d: L8a).

**R1a Observation declarations and per-world context.** Observation keys with required descriptions, counters, gauges, costs with budgets and quality levels, per-operation costs, the call-site log sink and the observation operations. It is the part of the observation model the line's bridges wait on, and it now also waits on the operation registry. Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), CL1 (Operations in Core). Value alone: Yes: every package can report, and how often stops depending on message text. 5 gates, 0.72 million tokens, estimated 2027-04-01 to 2027-04-08. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because R7a, L3, W1a wait on it.

**R1b Flight recorder, guards, intent key and ledger, measurements.** R1k is the intent key and the ledger, one per world in Core, which Networking stamps; R1b proper is the flight recorder, guards with policy, a native queue for jobs, the profiler mirror, the settings asset and key validator, and the measurements of the numbers table. The ledger's grace time is a number of seconds. Nine stories: the attack round added the ledger departure rule and its soak. The 3 and 6 split is mine. Depends on R1a (Observation declarations and per-wo...). Value alone: Yes. 9 gates, 1.27 million tokens, estimated 2027-05-12 to 2027-08-11. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Saboteer, and NW1, R6a, R4 wait on it. Parts: R1k, 3 gates, 2027-05-12 to 2027-05-17, after R1a; R1b, 6 gates, 2027-08-03 to 2027-08-11, after R1a, R1k.

**R2 Diagnostics.** The collector, the black box as text and as an editor window, the counter relay over Networking's hub, and the one overlay that replaces the five. The shipped recorder, its file and its relay are held for the prediction spike. The area places Diagnostics after Corsairs on the line and before any launch, so Saboteer's frame cost trace and CrewLogRelay stay until it lands. R2a is 4 gates and R2b 3; the split is mine. Depends on R1a (Observation declarations and per-wo...), R1b (Flight recorder, guards, intent key...), UI2 (StyledText, TextLog, TextEntry, Win...), NW2 (PurrNet adapter and first consumers). Value alone: Yes. 7 gates, 1.02 million tokens, estimated 2028-04-20 to 2028-05-01. Needed for: neither milestone; it follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch, and Saboteer's first Steam release waits for R2b because Saboteer installs Diagnostics. Human-only: Judge the overlay and the window. Parts: R2a, 4 gates, 2028-04-20 to 2028-04-25, after R1a, R1b, UI2; R2b, 3 gates, 2028-04-26 to 2028-05-01, after R2a, NW2.

**R3 Reference frames and riding.** Reference frames and riding: in Character.Body the ReferenceFrameBinding, the rider, the frame sources and motion claims, with the probe cost measured and Saboteer's four ride implementations deleted; the Character.Networking bridge; the Character.WorldObjects bridge, after L3's collision part; and the world-step order test, which waits for Networking's Simulate story. Runtime files twelve stories (the one that holds Core's noun is L2c's) and I use its split of 7, 3, 1 and 1. R3a is what Navigation's N6 waits for. Depends on L4 (StateMachine and Character), R1a (Observation declarations and per-wo...), R0 (Simulation vocabulary in Core), L2c (Core's ground vocabulary, section a...), NW2 (PurrNet adapter and first consumers), L3 (WorldObjects package), PWO (WorldObjects pitch with its spike), NW3s (Region streaming and the capacity r...). Value alone: Yes: four copies become one tested one. 12 gates, 1.72 million tokens, estimated 2027-08-19 to 2027-12-15. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because R4, R7b wait on it. Human-only: Designers add the frame sources in the inspector; play the gym. Parts: R3a, 7 gates, 2027-08-19 to 2027-08-30, after L4, R1a, R0, L2c; R3b, 3 gates, 2027-12-03 to 2027-12-07, after R3a, NW2; R3c, 1 gate, 2027-12-15 to 2027-12-15, after R3a, L3, PWO; R3d, 1 gate, 2027-12-15 to 2027-12-15, after R3a, NW3s.

**R4 Containment and the outside move seam.** Containment: the support gate, containment on a stride, the rescue ladder over a claim, Character.GeoForge and GeoForge's readiness query, and the outside move seam; the base floater and Corsairs' falling fix are deleted. Eleven stories: the claims round added IPlayableCharacter and CharacterKit.Body.IsPlayable, tested in edit mode. R4p, the playable marker on the controller, is the epic's first story and needs only Character, so ArtPipeline's A2 waits for it and not for the containment stories (a claim to Runtime). Depends on L4 (StateMachine and Character), R1b (Flight recorder, guards, intent key...), R3a (Reference frames and riding), L7a (GeoForge presenter seam), W2a (Density out). Value alone: Partly: the guard and ladder work without the gate. 11 gates, 1.51 million tokens, estimated 2027-08-02 to 2027-10-26. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because A2, R7b wait on it. Parts: R4p, 1 gate, 2027-08-02 to 2027-08-02, after L4; R4, 10 gates, 2027-10-12 to 2027-10-26, after R1b, R3a, L7a, W2a.

**R5a Character.Rig edit-mode surface.** Character.Rig's edit-mode surface: the rig contract asset with hold pose, rebinding of worn items, the ragdoll dry run, sockets resolved by id once at spawn, and the character operations. The contract has no integer version and gains a playable-character figure: the reference figure is a playable character's prefab. ArtPipeline's bridge calls these. Depends on L4 (StateMachine and Character). Value alone: Yes. 5 gates, 0.73 million tokens, estimated 2027-07-29 to 2027-08-04. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because A2, L11d1, A3 wait on it.

**R5b Ragdoll driver on claims (backlog).** The ragdoll driver on motion claims: knock-down, hips handoff, get-up, carry as a claim. Backlog: nothing on the line waits for it, so R7b's fault matrix covers the ragdoll only as far as R5a's dry run. Depends on R3a (Reference frames and riding), R5a (Character.Rig edit-mode surface), L4 (StateMachine and Character). Value alone: Partly: it needs R3's arbiter. 7 gates, 1.07 million tokens, backlog, not scheduled. Needed for: neither milestone: backlog, priced and not scheduled.

**R6 Inventory transactions.** R6a is the public transaction surface (receipts, commit, mint and burn, conservation guard and audit, intent replay), which Studio.Gameplay's equipment is built on in L11a1; the Owner source binding is stated and there is no escrow step, because Saboteer is owner-authoritative and a player's own inventory changes at once. R6b makes the raw writers internal (it merges to Inventory's followed branch only together with R6c, which starts right after it ends) and moves the treasure chest, the world item take and the hotbar swap onto transactions; it waits for L11a1 so the probe of the seams happens before the writers close, and for LV5b2. R6d, two stories, is the linked move across two writers: both halves under one intent key, each source marking itself dirty with that key, the open entry the audit counts, the owner's step committing on the click and the host's receipt, the return step on a refusal, and a test with two viewers under simulated latency and loss. The client never sees a half: Inventory.UITK draws the stack in its target slot at once and swaps it for the real item when the host's step replicates. It waits for R6b and for Networking's linked upload and hold (NW2). Depends on L6 (Inventory and Modifiers audit), R1b (Flight recorder, guards, intent key...), NW1 (Replication protocol), NW2 (PurrNet adapter and first consumers), L11a1 (Studio packages), LV5b2 (StrangersDev). Value alone: Yes. 10 gates, 1.38 million tokens, estimated 2027-09-24 to 2027-12-14. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Saboteer, and L11a1, LV5b2, R7b wait on it. Parts: R6a, 3 gates, 2027-09-24 to 2027-09-28, after L6, R1b, NW1, NW2; R6b, 5 gates, 2027-11-08 to 2027-11-15, after R6a, L11a1, LV5b2; R6d, 2 gates, 2027-12-13 to 2027-12-14, after R6b, NW2.

**R6c Corsairs onto transactions.** Corsairs' own conversion, after R6b and before R7c: PlayerBagEngine sort and merge as moves in one transaction, the save load as a mint under save.load, EquipmentInventory, the crafting console commands, and the player container's writer setting. Seven call sites in Corsairs write slots today. Depends on R6b (Inventory transactions). Value alone: No: it is Corsairs' own conversion; without it Corsairs stops compiling the day the raw writers close, and one of its writes sits in a sealed subclass of the engine that internal locks out. It is boosted to start right after R6b ends and to merge with it (reader finding 5); the scheduler had put it four months later. 5 gates, 0.68 million tokens, estimated 2027-11-16 to 2027-11-22. Needed for: Corsairs Isle on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Corsairs, and R7c waits on it.

**R7a Baseline soak.** The Areas scenario run against today's Saboteer with R1a's counters, so every later epic shows a before and after number. Four stories, as the area files them; the fourth is Saboteer's frame cost, worst frame and memory on the reference PC through the game's own FrameCostTrace, stored beside the soak counts. Whether the trace gives total memory is the story's first check. Depends on R1a (Observation declarations and per-wo...). Value alone: Yes: it counts the bugs that exist. 4 gates, 0.56 million tokens, estimated 2027-04-09 to 2027-04-14. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because L3, L4, R7b wait on it.

**R7b Full soak, gyms, fault matrix.** R7b is Saboteer's soak and fault matrix (withheld support, hitch, repeated intent, late joiner, save and load, a rider on a hull in a dedicated-server build), run headless through local Unity CLI test runs after every Saboteer-tagged rewire has landed; its fourth story reruns the frame figures against R7a's baseline with Saboteer's own trace, because Diagnostics comes later. R7b also waits for L8c, L8u and LV7d: a game sold on Steam is a customer from its first release (decided 2026-10-24), so Saboteer on the line means its players' saves keep loading. R7c is Corsairs' run after its last rewire, including R6c, plus one repeat of Saboteer's soak after UN6. The area counts 4 and 3 for R7b and R7c, and so do I. Its gym body is the soak's own playable prefab field until W4c lands, so R7b does not wait for the gym; it waits for R6d, the linked move, and its two-viewer test. Depends on L8c (Persistence: both games' stores con...), L8u (Persistence: upgrade of a shipped g...), LV7d (Gates), A4 (Rewire Saboteer's prefabs onto cont...), R3a (Reference frames and riding), R3b (Reference frames and riding), R3c (Reference frames and riding), R3d (Reference frames and riding), R4 (Containment and the outside move seam), R5a (Character.Rig edit-mode surface), R6b (Inventory transactions), R6d (Inventory transactions), R7a (Baseline soak), WF3a (Checks, adoption and the local gate), UI4b (Switchboard), UI5 (Tooltip host and bound settings rows), UI6b (Input contexts, layout as a Persist...), CL3a (Retire the old console, convert Sab...), NW4b (Events, operations and time), W10a (Boards and generators), W4b (Places layers), WF5a (The release train), L12 (Decals, Volumetrics, TetherShop to...), LV7a (Gates), L11a1 (Studio packages), UN6 (Blobs and hosts leave the games), WF7b (Games ship the same way), W2b (Density out), W7 (Near meets far), W9a (Lines, scatter, benchmark scene), W5 (Places in the world), W8 (Seed preview and labels), NW3b (Region streaming and the capacity r...), UN4b (WorldObjects store and awakening), UN5 (Instancing seam), W1b (The world model), L11a2 (Studio packages), L11d2 (Studio packages), R6c (Corsairs onto transactions), W10b (A board nested in a place). Value alone: Yes. 7 gates, 0.95 million tokens, estimated 2027-12-16 to 2028-04-19. Needed for: Saboteer on the line (and later classes for its other parts), because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Saboteer, and the first release (LV6m, WF5m) waits on it. Human-only: Watch the hull test. Parts: R7b, 4 gates, 2027-12-16 to 2027-12-21, after L8c, L8u, LV7d, A4, R3a, R3b, R3c, R3d, R4, R5a, R6b, R6d, R7a, WF3a, UI4b, UI5, UI6b, CL3a, NW4b, W10a, W4b, WF5a, L12, LV7a, L11a1; R7c, 3 gates, 2028-04-17 to 2028-04-19, after R7b, UN6, WF7b, W2b, W7, W9a, W5, W8, NW3b, UN4b, UN5, W1b, L11a2, L11d2, R6c, W10b.

**R8 WorldObjects operations, object layer validator, authoring operations.** R8a: WorldObjects' presets, spawn, find, inspect and remove operations and the object layer validator. R8b: the two authoring operations (a preset from a model, an item definition from a preset); both write drafts only, and a person approves them into a named folder. The split 3 and 2 is mine. Depends on L3 (WorldObjects package), CL1 (Operations in Core), L6 (Inventory and Modifiers audit). Value alone: Yes. 5 gates, 0.71 million tokens, estimated 2027-12-16 to 2027-12-22. ### UI and text

**UI1 UI split, truthful package, UIKit, theme validator, TabView.** UI becomes Widgets, Screens and Tests; the menu chrome and the studio theme land in Studio.Presentation in the same epic; the package declares Core; samples move to the samples folder; game shapes leave; the slot styles go to Inventory; Unity's own TabView replaces the tab widgets; theme variants and serialized view identity. Two of the eleven stories are the restructures of UI and Chat (Living's claim, accepted at checkpoint 3; the UI owner's nine do not include them). The Studio.Presentation folder in StudioDev is one story that waits for L11t; it is split off as UI1b so the console does not wait for it. UI1's assembly split is the breaking merge, so it moves in front of the freeze. Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), LV1b (Layout and probes), L11t (Studio packages). Value alone: Yes: a stranger can install UI without the Input System and without Chat. 11 gates, 1.69 million tokens, estimated 2026-11-11 to 2028-01-04. Needed for: the cut-over and the demo before the freeze (and later classes for its other parts), because it is part of the cut-over that lands before the freeze (answer 23) or of the demo's files, and UI2, UI4a1, UI6a wait on it. Parts: UI1, 10 gates, 2026-11-11 to 2026-11-25, after L1, LV1b; UI1b, 1 gate, 2028-01-04 to 2028-01-04, after UI1, L11t.

**UI2 StyledText, TextLog, TextEntry, WindowFrame.** Styled text with template spans, the text log, text entry and the window frame, with edit-mode logic tests and play-mode element tests; the Gallery sample shows them; the log's flush cost is measured with 10,000 lines. Depends on UI1 (UI split, truthful package, UIKit,...). Value alone: Yes: both games can replace their copies of the focus code. 9 gates, 1.34 million tokens, estimated 2026-12-03 to 2026-12-16. Needed for: the cut-over and the demo before the freeze, because it is part of the cut-over that lands before the freeze (answer 23) or of the demo's files, and UI3, UI4a2 wait on it.

**UI3 ConsolePanel and ConsoleView over the operation list.** The console panel and view over the operation directory with completion, audience filter, history and the scoped log sink, and the public result-to-lines helper; the views are deleted here (Core's uGUI console and its prefabs, and Chat's console); the old registries, attributes, router and ConsoleLogger go in CL3b, and CL3a, Saboteer's conversion, waits for this epic. It lands before the freeze, so the demo shows the new console. Depends on UI2 (StyledText, TextLog, TextEntry, Win...), CL1 (Operations in Core). Value alone: Yes: one console replaces three. 6 gates, 0.97 million tokens, estimated 2026-12-17 to 2027-01-05. Needed for: the cut-over and the demo before the freeze, because it is part of the cut-over that lands before the freeze (answer 23) or of the demo's files, and CL3a, UI4a wait on it.

**UI4 Switchboard.** Switchboard, the networked text package under its placeholder name. U4a, 11 stories, lands before the freeze and the roadmap places it in three parts: UI4a1, 5 stories, the library (profile, kinds, scopes, session, host, filters, rate limit, mute list, templates, history, the in-process loopback); UI4a2, 5 stories, Switchboard.UI with ChatView, the UI bridge and the chat settings page; and UI4a, 1 story, Saboteer's cut-over (its own profile asset, its ChatNetwork kept thin as the client interface's implementation, its input contexts class, the console and chat views in place of its own, and its chat window, settings panel and hand-wired switches deleted). The split of the 11 into 5, 5 and 1 is mine, by the owner's own line counts (about 2,500 of 4,900 lines in the library, the cut-over named as one story); a claim to UI. U4b, 4 stories, is Switchboard.Networking over the protocol assembly and the region scope, which retires Saboteer's thin ChatNetwork; it waits for Networking's event channel (NW4a) and the check seam (CL4), and joins Saboteer's class with N4a. ### World

**W1 The world model.** The world model behind WorldForgeKit: surfaces, operators, rules, footprint sampling, occupancy, a plan without statics, no Entities in WorldForge's 35 runtime files, derived ids, a detect-only fingerprint, estimate and rasters. Today's output is frozen as golden hashes first (W1g) and the 124 tests ported, with no old-format reader. W1g is 1 gate, W1a 7 (stories 1 to 8, Saboteer's class, which W2 and W10a wait on) and W1b 12, with the WorldForge internals pitch between; the new story in W1b is the compare that prints one seed's golden region hashes so the Unity 6.7 probe and the one Unity 7 alpha copy list every difference. Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), L2c (Core's ground vocabulary, section a...), R0 (Simulation vocabulary in Core), R1a (Observation declarations and per-wo...), CL4 (Authoring contract and check seam), PW (WorldForge internals pitch). Value alone: Yes: seed preview, maps and plan work without a voxel. 20 gates, 3.47 million tokens, estimated 2027-04-14 to 2028-02-25. Needed for: Saboteer on the line (and later classes for its other parts), because L3, W2a, W10a wait on it. Parts: W1g, 1 gate, 2027-04-14 to 2027-04-14, after nothing; W1a, 7 gates, 2027-05-03 to 2027-05-11, after L1, L2c, R0, R1a, CL4, W1g; W1b, 12 gates, 2028-02-09 to 2028-02-25, after W1a, PW.

**W2 Density out.** W2.0 is the spike planning story for Pro's nine passes, ending in a go or no-go that gates Saboteer's boards. W2a: the density source seam and public encoding in GeoForge, point queries, a camera-free versioned run-list read, the change and residency event, chunk interest over Core's viewer source, the presenter split into a collision part and a picture part, the manager as an instance; the world instance steps the collision scene. W2b, 12 stories: the WorldForge-to-GeoForge bridge, occupancy skipping, Pro's pipeline retired for Corsairs and the edit store synced. W2c, the last two stories: the blob tables become plain structs and arrays and the three fillers are deleted, which must wait for W10a's last story, the one that removes GeoForge Pro from Saboteer's manifest, because Pro's nine passes compile against both and Saboteer follows the branch (decided 2026-10-24, answer 19). W2 does not edit MaterialSideTable (UI7c moves it). W2a waits for NV1, Navigation's spike, so its go or no-go can shape the run-list read and the change event before both games depend on them. The owner files W2 as Saboteer's class; I keep W2a there and place W2b and W2c in Corsairs' class, because W10a needs only W2a (a claim to World). Depends on W1a (The world model), L7a (GeoForge presenter seam), NV1 (Navigation spike), NW2 (PurrNet adapter and first consumers), W10aL (Boards and generators). Value alone: Yes: the gate is the Corsairs budgets unchanged. 29 gates, 4.56 million tokens, estimated 2027-05-18 to 2028-03-01. Needed for: W20 and W2a are Saboteer on the line, because W4b, W10a and R4 wait on W2a; W2b and W2c are Corsairs Isle on the line, and W2c waits for W10a's last story. Human-only: The developer captures the Corsairs F2 numbers with the bridge in place. Parts: W20, 1 gate, 2027-05-18 to 2027-05-18, after nothing; W2a, 14 gates, 2027-05-19 to 2027-06-08, after W1a, L7a, W20, NV1; W2b, 12 gates, 2028-01-12 to 2028-01-28, after W2a, NW2; W2c, 2 gates, 2028-02-29 to 2028-03-01, after W2b, W10aL.

**W3 Objects out.** Placement records with derived ids into WorldObjects, decoration stampers as rules, the static scratch lists gone, and WorldForge stops emitting entity spawn requests (UN4 deletes the old path after UN3's go). Depends on W1a (The world model), L3 (WorldObjects package), NW1 (Replication protocol). Value alone: Yes. 8 gates, 1.22 million tokens, estimated 2028-01-05 to 2028-01-14. Needed for: Corsairs Isle on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Corsairs, and W9s, W7 wait on it.

**W4a Places core.** The Place asset with typed layers, owners, composition from Saboteer's solver and six sockets, nesting, duplicate, validation and preview, per-cell rules, and the public read surface for checks (harvestable layers, entrance, rules). The first story measures whether the adjacency solver converges with many cells fixed, on Saboteer's real parts. A Place's data is Unity serialization, with packing for saves and the wire. Fifteen stories; the first proves that a layer whose type is missing survives a save of the asset. Depends on CL4 (Authoring contract and check seam), L3 (WorldObjects package), L2c (Core's ground vocabulary, section a...). **W4c Places gym and checks.** places gym-start, gym-status and gym-leave over a Place or a prefab asset, with the playable character of the game (the project names the prefab; Studio.Content's profile offers the studio's, and the studio profile is edited in StudioDev only), the stable character name and the held prop, the cell grid with a reroll-seed preview, and a farming-village stranger step run on the default profile. Decided on 2026-10-24 (answer 2 of checkpoint 4): the gym sits behind Saboteer's milestone and before any Corsairs launch, so the program refuses a start before every Saboteer-class part has ended; it stays in front of A7b (Open in gym) and W9b. The gym walk-through carries no navigation line until Navigation lands. Its held-item story needs a packaged character and its hold pose, so it waits, through L11d1, for L4 and R5a. Value in the soak: none, since R7b uses its own playable prefab field until the gym lands. Depends on W4a (Places core), W4b (Places layers), UI7a (Core.Editor controls), L11d1 (Studio packages). It runs behind Saboteer's milestone and before any Corsairs launch (answer 2 of checkpoint 4); A7b, W9b and CL8 wait on it, and no Corsairs rewire does, so it is no Corsairs seed (reader finding 12). Human-only: Play a place in the gym; judge the cell-grid inspector.

Needed for: Corsairs Isle on the line (and later classes for its other parts), because W7 waits on it. Human-only: Judge the seam by eye in the test scene. ### Networking

**NW1 Replication protocol.** The engine-only protocol assembly: contract, dirty set, counted sections, sequence and gaps, snapshot pager, batch ring, join scheduler, interest map with the audience reach bound, lease ids, stamping of Core's intent key, WritePolicy and the owner-mode path (a configuration per replicated thing with no privileged default; owner mode needs no validator), and the in-memory simulator with hostile-command and join-race cases. No PurrNet type appears in it. Twelve stories. Depends on PK (Packing slice), R0 (Simulation vocabulary in Core), R1a (Observation declarations and per-wo...), R1k (Flight recorder, guards, intent key...), L2c (Core's ground vocabulary, section a...). Value alone: Partly: a tested protocol with no consumer yet. 12 gates, 1.90 million tokens, estimated 2027-06-09 to 2027-06-28. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because NW4a, NW2, W4b wait on it.

**NW2 PurrNet adapter and first consumers.** The replication hub and region on PurrNet, the pooled byte-data carrier, the three write policies with an unset one refused by the settings check, the owner-mode relay in the adapter, resync, host handling, GeoForge.Networking over the protocol assembly, Saboteer's terrain module (534 lines and a defect list) and the GeoForge sample module replaced. Two stories carry the linked move: the hold (a batch names its linked partner and a client holds a half for at most one tick) and the linked upload (one message carrying step 1's records and step 2's packed call, applied by the host in one step), with the second-process latency test. The tick rate and the fixed step are settings read once, 20 and 50 as the starting values, and nothing is derived from either (decided 2026-10-24). NW2g, the gate story (the PURRNET define, the constraints and the bootstrap that offers the install, proved first as a probe), stands in front of the freeze with N4a; the other twelve are the consumer stories and land after the festival, outside the code freeze. The owner's claim list says 12 and its epic table 13; I price the table. Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), NW1 (Replication protocol), L7a (GeoForge presenter seam). Value alone: Yes: Saboteer drops 534 lines and a defect list. 13 gates, 2.28 million tokens, estimated 2027-01-15 to 2027-07-28. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Saboteer, and NW4a, R6a, LV5b1 wait on it. Human-only: Define the telemetry-off symbol; make the region prefab; watch a two-process test. Parts: NW2g, 1 gate, 2027-01-15 to 2027-01-15, after L1; NW2, 12 gates, 2027-07-12 to 2027-07-28, after NW1, L7a, NW2g.

**NW3 Region streaming and the capacity report.** NW3s is the story that reads the manual on PhysicsScene.Simulate from a late FixedUpdate and runs it in a second scene (my split, one gate: Runtime's order test waits for it). NW3a: world scope end to end, a synthetic-viewer benchmark, the PurrNet whitelist and spawn cost measured, the capacity report, a two-world smoke test whose falling body uses the world instance's step driver. NW3b: WorldObjects.Networking, and Corsairs' three modules (2,575 lines), proximity job and 49-region respawn retired. Eleven stories, two fewer than the fix round counted, because the far layer pieces moved to NW9. Depends on NW2 (PurrNet adapter and first consumers), L3 (WorldObjects package), UN4a (WorldObjects store and awakening). Value alone: Yes: the first eight give a measured streamer; the last three remove Corsairs' modules. 11 gates, 1.63 million tokens, estimated 2027-12-09 to 2028-04-07. Needed for: Saboteer on the line (and later classes for its other parts), because R3d waits on it. Human-only: Measure a thousand colliders in a real dedicated-server build. Parts: NW3s, 1 gate, 2027-12-09 to 2027-12-09, after NW2; NW3a, 7 gates, 2028-02-29 to 2028-03-09, after NW3s; NW3b, 3 gates, 2028-04-05 to 2028-04-07, after NW3a, L3, UN4a.

**NW4 Events, operations and time.** NW4a, 4 stories, in front of the freeze: the event channel with audiences (Participants, Region, Group, All) over PurrNet's player broadcast, the audience reach bound and the event backstop place, and a two-peer test that carries a Saboteer chat line. It waits for NW2g and for Core's clock; its first story reads whether the broadcast calls exist in the PurrNet version Saboteer already runs. U4b, the networking bridge of Switchboard (UI's story), retires Saboteer's thin ChatNetwork and joins this class (the UI owner files it as Corsairs'; the networking owner asks for Saboteer's, and UI's own note says it joins if N4 is Saboteer's). NW4b, 5 stories: the operation gateway with the audience enforced, `PurrNetTickSource` and `ServerClock` over Core's `TickClock`, the routing of a write by the policy of the source that holds its target, the two-build test, and the retirement of Corsairs' per-tick time request; its operation stories wait for CL1b (the router seam). Saboteer's CrewLogRelay stays until the shipped recorder exists. Depends on NW1 (Replication protocol), NW2g (PurrNet adapter and first consumers), CL1 (Operations in Core), UI3 (ConsolePanel and ConsoleView over t...), R0 (Simulation vocabulary in Core), CL1b (Operations in Core). Value alone: Yes: the chat corruption and the per-tick time request go. 9 gates, 1.35 million tokens, estimated 2027-06-29 to 2027-12-03. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Saboteer, and UI4b, R7b, LV5c0 wait on it. Parts: NW4a, 4 gates, 2027-06-29 to 2027-07-02, after NW1, NW2g, CL1, UI3, R0; NW4b, 5 gates, 2027-11-29 to 2027-12-03, after NW1, NW4a, CL1b, R0.

Depends on NW2 (PurrNet adapter and first consumers), CL4 (Authoring contract and check seam), LV2 (Runner and read tier). **NW6 Delta codecs (deferred).** An adapter that registers Core's pure delta pack and unpack with PurrNet for unreliable per-entity streams; the per-player ledger is deferred. Built only if a measurement asks. Depends on NW2 (PurrNet adapter and first consumers), PK (Packing slice). Value alone: No. 3 gates, 0.44 million tokens, backlog, not scheduled. Needed for: neither milestone: backlog, priced and not scheduled.

Nothing is built from it. Depends on NW2 (PurrNet adapter and first consumers). 1 gate, 0.18 million tokens, estimated 2028-09-25 to 2028-09-25. Depends on NW3a (Region streaming and the capacity r...), W6n (Horizon draws), W6a (Horizon draws). **NW10 Stepping ownership when a world predicts.** One story, a probe in NetworkingDev: one scene holding PurrDiction's PredictionManager and one scene stepped by a driver, asserting that each local physics scene is stepped once, reading Physics.simulationMode and Time.fixedDeltaTime after the manager starts, and a written rule for the driver (it leaves a scene that holds a manager alone, and steps the default scene when the process is in script mode), filed to Runtime and WorldObjects.
