Parts: LV5a, 2 gates, 2027-09-22 to 2027-09-23, after L1, LV2; LV5m, 1 gate, 2028-05-10 to 2028-05-10, after LV5a, UN4b; LV5s, 1 gate, 2027-09-29 to 2027-09-29, after LV5a, UI1; LV5b1, 1 gate, 2027-10-11 to 2027-10-11, after LV5s, L4, UI2, L8, NW2, UI4a; LV5b2, 2 gates, 2027-10-13 to 2027-10-14, after LV5b1, L3, L4, L6, L8, R6a; LV5b3, 1 gate, 2027-12-22 to 2027-12-22, after LV5b2, W4a; LV5c0, 1 gate, 2028-01-03 to 2028-01-03, after LV5a, L3, L6, L8, NW2, NW4b, R6a; LV5c, 2 gates, 2028-05-29 to 2028-05-30, after LV5b1, L12, W6b, NV2a, L7a, R2a, L11b, UI4b, W4b, NW3a, L11a1, W2a; LV5d, 2 gates, 2028-05-31 to 2028-06-01, after LV5c.

**LV6 The Spellbound window: Line and Package pages.** The Spellbound window's Line and Package pages, installing and updating from git URLs; the package list comes from GitHub. Branch mode: #beta then #main, Update re-adds the line, and a known-good tag is shown as advice, never a pin. Depends on CL1 (Operations in Core), LV2 (Runner and read tier), WF4a (Releases from commits), UI1 (UI split, truthful package, UIKit,...). Value alone: Yes: installing Core and seeing the line. 8 gates, 1.59 million tokens, estimated 2028-07-13 to 2028-07-25. Needed for: neither milestone; it follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch.

**LV6m Window maintenance and branch modes.** The window's three modes on a slice that needs no catalogue: the committed project setting (tags, branch, maintenance), the manifest writer for #beta, #main and #release-<major> over the installed packages, and Update re-adding the line. New at checkpoint 5 (decided 2026-10-24, answers 4 and 5): a buyer installs a released tag, our games follow a branch, and a shipped game follows the maintenance branch of its Core major. Dated to Saboteer's first Steam release itself: it waits for every Saboteer-tagged part, for Corsairs' soak and for the first stable tags, because from this day Saboteer follows `release-<major>`, where a pull request that removes a public line fails (reader findings 1, 2, 6, 7, 8); until it lands the manifest lines are edited by hand, which is the same text. Its first step confirms the settings mechanism and that the package manager exposes the installed commit. Depends on LV2 (Runner and read tier), WF5a (The release train), R7b and R7c (Full soak, gyms, fault matrix), every Saboteer-tagged part, CT (Core 1.0), LV7c (Gates), WF5b (The release train), R2b (Diagnostics), L11b and L11c (Studio packages). Value alone: Yes. 1 gate, 0.18 million tokens, estimated 2028-06-05 to 2028-06-05. Needed for: nothing after it; it is the first release and in no milestone's class. Human-only: Open each game once on the maintenance branch.

**LV7 Gates.** LV7a: the push gate, the floor compile and the 6.3 row, which no longer wait for the move to 6.7. LV7b and LV7c: the tag gate and the release checklist, dated now because Saboteer's first Steam release needs the first stable tags (reader finding 8). LV7d, two stories: the stored-format release gate, because a game sold on Steam is a customer from its first release built on the line, and the release scan, which fails a release when any shipped text file starts with a %UNITY directive newer than the oldest supported editor (decided 2026-10-24, answer 25). It is in Saboteer's class and R7b waits for it, together with L8u, so the guarantee exists before Saboteer's first Steam release (reader finding 9). Depends on LV3a (Operations and compiled rules), WF3a (Checks, adoption and the local gate), WF4b (Releases from commits), LV5b2 (StrangersDev), LV5c0 (StrangersDev), R7b (Full soak, gyms, fault matrix), LV5m (StrangersDev), LV5d (StrangersDev), LV3b (Operations and compiled rules), L8 (Persistence rewrite). Value alone: Yes. 5 gates, 0.88 million tokens, estimated 2027-11-23 to 2028-06-02. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Saboteer, and R7b waits on it. Parts: LV7a, 3 gates, 2027-11-23 to 2027-11-25, after LV3a, WF3a; LV7b, 2 gates, 2028-05-11 to 2028-05-15, after LV7a, WF4b, LV5b2, LV5c0, R7b, LV5m; LV7c, 1 gate, 2028-06-02 to 2028-06-02, after LV7b, LV5d; LV7d, 2 gates, 2027-12-08 to 2027-12-09, after LV3b, L8, LV7a.


### Workflow

**WF1 One convention, one board.** One convention, one board: the Area options, fields and report script, the commit subject rule, the issue forms and the artist onboarding form, the reusable workflow that opens a pull request from a pushed art branch, the GitHub App the organisation owns for the release and pull request Actions, and the settings story (protected default branch, required unity-tests status, code owner review on the judgment fields, line-text-address.txt with its copy test). Thirteen stories: WF1a 2, WF1b 10, and WF1r, the last, which switches the artist ruleset on in each game repository in the sitting where art's A5a lands there, so the program holds it within a week of A5a. A5a in turn needs WF1b's App, Action and protected main first (stated both ways). Depends on A5a (Send and Get latest). Value alone: Yes, the day it lands for the team. 13 gates, 1.71 million tokens, estimated 2027-01-27 to 2027-03-18. Needed for: the cut-over and the demo before the freeze (and later classes for its other parts), because WF2b, WF7a wait on it. Human-only: Open question: App or personal token. Parts: WF1a, 2 gates, 2027-01-27 to 2027-01-28, after nothing; WF1b, 10 gates, 2027-01-29 to 2027-02-16, after WF1a; WF1r, 1 gate, 2027-03-18 to 2027-03-18, after A5a.

**WF2 Templates.** Templates: skeleton, initialise, setup and callers, and the template files. The template carries line-text-address.txt and a branch-named PackageCheck manifest line; skeleton-version.txt becomes a record, not a held version. The Packages/<name> probe is LV1's first story, counted once. Depends on LV1a (Layout and probes), WF1a (One convention, one board). Value alone: Yes: new packages and games can start from it. 8 gates, 1.08 million tokens, estimated 2026-11-10 to 2027-02-09. Needed for: the cut-over and the demo before the freeze, because UI4a1, WF7a wait on it. Human-only: Try `setup.sh` on a clean Windows machine and a Mac. Parts: WF2a, 4 gates, 2026-11-10 to 2026-11-16, after LV1a; WF2b, 4 gates, 2027-02-03 to 2027-02-09, after WF2a, WF1a.

**WF3 Checks, adoption and the local gate.** Checks, adoption and the local gate: the title check, the Unity jobs and the local gate, which runs packagecheck surface for the stored-format hash. The test runner spike (one story) is undated; the author runs the Unity tests before review (decided). Depends on WF1b (One convention, one board), WF2b (Templates), LV2 (Runner and read tier), LV3a (Operations and compiled rules). Value alone: Partly: the title check works at once, the Unity jobs wait on Living. 9 gates, 1.23 million tokens, estimated 2027-09-29 to 2028-08-21. Needed for: Saboteer on the line (and later classes for its other parts), because WF4a, LV7a, R7b wait on it. Human-only: Type the post-status line; agree merge blocking on private repos. Parts: WF3a, 4 gates, 2027-09-29 to 2027-10-05, after WF1b, WF2b, LV2; WF3b, 5 gates, 2028-08-14 to 2028-08-21, after WF3a, LV3a.

**WF3s Test runner spike.** The test runner spike: whether a CI runner is worth having beyond local Unity CLI runs. Not required for the plan. Depends on WF3a (Checks, adoption and the local gate). Value alone: Yes: a written answer. 1 gate, 0.13 million tokens, undated, after Corsairs on the line. Needed for: neither game: undated, after Corsairs on the line.

**WF4 Releases from commits.** Releases from commits: semantic-release and commitlint in CI only, beta and release-candidate branches, the first release, and the final tag mechanism. Nothing is pinned: games follow branches. Depends on WF3a (Checks, adoption and the local gate), LV5a (StrangersDev). Value alone: Yes. 12 gates, 1.60 million tokens, estimated 2027-10-18 to 2028-05-09. Needed for: Saboteer on the line (and later classes for its other parts), because WF5a waits on it. Human-only: A person merges beta into rc and fast-forwards main at the first stable release. Parts: WF4a, 6 gates, 2027-10-18 to 2027-10-25, after WF3a; WF4b, 6 gates, 2028-05-02 to 2028-05-09, after WF4a, LV5a.

**WF5 The release train.** The release train, six stories: release order (Core first), one command for a line release, and the line release cutting and protecting `release-<major>` in every repository when a Core major's tags are made (WF5a, 3); the pair matrix as a release artifact, the candidate tag starting the StrangersDev run, and drift-report.sh (WF5b, 2); and WF5m, the maintenance branch for a shipped major: its entry in the release configuration, the pull request guard that refuses a breaking change or a removed surface line, and the check that a shipped game's manifest line names it. Decided on 2026-10-24 (answer 4): a shipped game follows the maintenance branch of its Core major, so WF5m is dated to Saboteer's first Steam release built on the line, after every Saboteer-tagged part and the first stable tags; the program holds it within a week of LV6m. consumers.txt and the rolling bump pull request are removed. Depends on WF4a (Releases from commits), WF4b (Releases from commits), LV5b1 (StrangersDev), R7b (Full soak, gyms, fault matrix). Value alone: Yes, after WF4. 6 gates, 0.80 million tokens, estimated 2027-12-06 to 2028-06-06. Needed for: WF5a is Saboteer on the line; WF5m is the first Steam release and in no milestone's class; WF5b follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch. Human-only: The drift report is run by hand. Parts: WF5a, 3 gates, 2027-12-06 to 2027-12-08, after WF4a; WF5b, 2 gates, 2028-05-11 to 2028-05-15, after WF5a, WF4b, LV5b1; WF5m, 1 gate, 2028-06-06 to 2028-06-06, after WF5a, R7b and the first-release list (every Saboteer-tagged part, R7c, CT, LV7c, WF5b, R2b, L11b, L11c).

**WF6 Selling.** Store packing, the listing generator, a licence pass, docs publishing, the support routine and the bug button. Undated, after both games; publisher enrolment is the developer's own story and not a gate. Depends on WF4a (Releases from commits), CL2 (Pipeline bridge and spellbound_run), WF4b (Releases from commits). Value alone: Yes, per package that is listed. 7 gates, 0.93 million tokens, undated, after Corsairs on the line. Needed for: neither game: undated, after Corsairs on the line. Human-only: Enrolment (five to seven business days), the store upload, the legal name, namespace and pricing. Parts: WF6a, 4 gates, undated, after WF4a, CL2; WF6b, 3 gates, undated, after WF6a, WF4b.

**WF7a Saboteer's demo build files.** The demo build's files and the tag script in Saboteer (the template files, the title rule, protected main), touching no game code and no scene, plus the freeze rule filed as a Decision issue a person closes with text: a change to a branch Saboteer follows that can break its build waits on a side branch inside the freeze and merges after Next Fest. Three stories. It is Saboteer-tagged, so it may not run in the freeze; the cut-over takes the whole window before it (answer 23), so this epic lands after the festival and the demo tag is made by hand. The Steam deploy and the tag are the developer's. Depends on WF1b (One convention, one board), WF2b (Templates). Value alone: Yes. 3 gates, 0.39 million tokens, estimated 2027-03-29 to 2027-03-31. Needed for: the demo's files; the window is full, so under this plan it lands after the festival and the demo is tagged by hand. Human-only: Choose the tag and run the deploy.

**WF7b Games ship the same way.** After Next Fest: Corsairs adopts the template files and the title rule and normalises its bundle version; both games replace ParrelSync with Multiplayer Play Mode and hold the art pull request caller; tag-release.sh attaches the build folder's file list to the release and prints whether the C++ runtime files of a Windows 6.7 build are in it, so the Steam upload includes them or the copy is switched off deliberately (the choice is MV's, found first by U9's second story); and pre-deploy-check.sh in GameTemplate runs Living's release scan before BuildPipeline's deploy runs steamcmd. Five stories. The first release (LV6m, WF5m) waits for it, so the release scan runs before the first Steam upload (reader finding 11). Depends on WF1b (One convention, one board), WF2b (Templates), WF7a (Saboteer's demo build files), R7b (Full soak, gyms, fault matrix), LV7d (Gates). Value alone: Yes. 5 gates, 0.66 million tokens, estimated 2028-04-03 to 2028-04-07. Needed for: Corsairs Isle on the line, because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Corsairs, and R7c waits on it.

**WF8 Rituals.** The scheduled Unity release review issue, a per-sprint line review issue and a retrospective form. Depends on WF1b (One convention, one board), UN1a (The Unity ledger). Value alone: Yes. 3 gates, 0.40 million tokens, estimated 2028-09-14 to 2028-09-19. Needed for: neither milestone; it follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch.


### Studio and pitches

**PWO WorldObjects pitch with its spike.** The WorldObjects pitch the developer asked for before L3: three gates of pitch (the world instance, the presenter, change and resolve, ids, the collision part in every build) and one spike story that tests the riskiest seam on a copy before the package is cut. It decides the shape of L3 and of R3's last story. Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), R0 (Simulation vocabulary in Core). Value alone: Yes: it decides the build. 4 gates, 1.41 million tokens, estimated 2027-04-08 to 2027-04-13. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because L3, R3c wait on it. Human-only: Review and approve the pitch; judge the spike's result.

**PP Persistence pitch with its spike.** The Persistence pitch the developer asked for before L8: three gates of pitch (the storage seam, the section attribute's use and the section header, the capture contract with a per-tick budget, a database implementation that never blocks a tick, and the upgrade of a shipped game's old saves, decided 2026-10-24: built before the first ship to a customer) and one spike story. It sizes L8 and L8b. It moves to the first weeks of the plan, because the first slice of Persistence that UI's chat settings page and layout section need (L8a) waits for it; the developer approves the pitch in November. Depends on L2c (Core's ground vocabulary, section a...). Value alone: Yes: it decides the build. 4 gates, 1.41 million tokens, estimated 2026-11-26 to 2026-12-02. Needed for: the cut-over and the demo before the freeze, because L8a waits on it. Human-only: Review and approve the pitch; judge the spike's result.

**L11 Studio packages.** StudioDev with its five packages: Studio.Tools first (the shell the ledger check, the art destination and the house rules need), then Gameplay (equipment, talents, the house set of states, tooltip wording, after its pitch), Online, Presentation and Content as the libraries they compile against land. Studio.Content holds only what both games share and never names a game; the shared gym profile is its first story. The profile is split: L11d1 holds the studio character and its hold pose, so it waits for L4 and R5a and a named item can be held, and the commands that grant abilities are L11d2's, which waits for L11a1. UI1 creates the Studio.Presentation package folder in StudioDev (L11t holds the repo) and UI4a the Studio.Online one; L11c and L11b are what remains of them. Eleven stories: one added for the house set. Studio.Online (L11b) waits for Diagnostics (R2b), which the area places after Corsairs on the line, so it leaves Corsairs' class (a claim to the line: its row says Online waits on L9 and L10). Studio.Content is edited in StudioDev and never inside a game (decided 2026-10-24, answer 18). Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), WF2a (Templates), LV1b (Layout and probes), PG (Studio.Gameplay pitch), L4 (StateMachine and Character), L6 (Inventory and Modifiers audit), R6a (Inventory transactions), UI5 (Tooltip host and bound settings rows), NW2 (PurrNet adapter and first consumers), R2b (Diagnostics), UI4b (Switchboard), L8 (Persistence rewrite), UI6b (Input contexts, layout as a Persist...), UI1b (UI split, truthful package, UIKit,...), W4b (Places layers), R5a (Character.Rig edit-mode surface). Value alone: Yes, cluster by cluster. 11 gates, 1.72 million tokens, estimated 2027-01-18 to 2028-05-16. Needed for: Saboteer on the line (and later classes for its other parts), because the area says it rewires, deletes code from or moves Saboteer, and A5a, W10a, LV3b wait on it. Parts: L11t, 2 gates, 2027-01-18 to 2027-01-19, after L1, WF2a, LV1b; L11a1, 3 gates, 2027-10-08 to 2027-10-12, after PG, L4, L6, R6a; L11a2, 1 gate, 2028-04-10 to 2028-04-10, after L11a1, UI5; L11b, 2 gates, 2028-05-04 to 2028-05-05, after NW2, R2b, UI4b; L11c, 1 gate, 2028-05-16 to 2028-05-16, after L8, UI6b, UI1b; L11d1, 1 gate, 2027-09-02 to 2027-09-02, after W4b, L11t, L4, R5a; L11d2, 1 gate, 2028-04-13 to 2028-04-13, after L11a1, L11a2, L11d1.

**PG Studio.Gameplay pitch.** The Studio.Gameplay pitch no area wrote: equipment, skills and talents as Studio compositions of Inventory's and Modifiers' public surface, and the tooltip lines. It follows L6, so it designs against the seams that exist. The Inventory and Modifiers pitch itself is removed; L6 is the audit. Depends on L6 (Inventory and Modifiers audit). Value alone: Yes: it decides the build. 3 gates, 1.28 million tokens, estimated 2027-09-24 to 2027-09-28. Needed for: Saboteer on the line, because L11a1 waits on it. **PW WorldForge internals pitch.** The deeper pitch the line left for WorldForge's internals: the generation model the world model's boundary seams were drawn for. It follows W1's first half and precedes the second. Depends on W1a (The world model). Value alone: Yes: it decides the build. 3 gates, 1.28 million tokens, estimated 2028-01-27 to 2028-01-31. Needed for: Corsairs Isle on the line, because W1b waits on it. Human-only: Review and approve the pitch.

Instancing draws with Unity's BatchRendererGroup only. Depends on UN3 (Batch path measurement), UN5 (Instancing seam). Value alone: Yes: it decides the build. 3 gates, 1.28 million tokens, estimated 2028-06-29 to 2028-07-03. Needed for: neither milestone; it follows Corsairs on the line and comes before any launch. It is a step to Core 1.0, and Saboteer's first Steam release waits for Core 1.0, so it is dated (reader finding 8). Depends on L1 (Core as assemblies), PK (Packing slice), L2c (Core's ground vocabulary, section a...), CL1 (Operations in Core), CL2 (Pipeline bridge and spellbound_run), CL4 (Authoring contract and check seam), CL5 (AI context), R0 (Simulation vocabulary in Core), R1a (Observation declarations and per-wo...), R1b (Flight recorder, guards, intent key...), UN2 (Unity's pool and the registry asset), UN4b (WorldObjects store and awakening), UN5 (Instancing seam). **CT Core 1.0.** The Core 1.0 tag. After it Core changes only additively; a breaking change waits for a major, at most once per Unity LTS cycle. Dated, since Saboteer's first Steam release waits for it (reader finding 8). Depends on AUc (Core's audit before Core 1.0), UN4b (WorldObjects store and awakening), UN2 (Unity's pool and the registry asset). Value alone: Yes. 1 gate, 0.15 million tokens, estimated 2028-05-16 to 2028-05-16. Needed for: Saboteer's first Steam release.

**SF Saboteer festival: ship the demo, demo defects, festival fixes, wrap up.** The four festival gates on fixed days, estimated and moved with whatever Steam's partner page says: SFa on 2027-02-03 ships the demo build, SFd on 2027-02-09 handles defects before the press preview (2027-02-11), SFb on 2027-02-24 fixes what the festival shows, and SFc on 2027-03-02 wraps up and re-tests. A fix to a package Saboteer uses is made on that package's branch, and nothing is pinned. 4 gates, 0.51 million tokens. Needed for: Saboteer's festival. Human-only: Choose the build, run the Steam deploy and the tag.

### Rounds and audit

Depends on the round it follows. 18 gates, 2.86 million tokens, estimated 2027-04-15 to 2028-10-09. Needed for: nothing waits on it; it keeps the rounds honest. ## Reserve

One working day per stream in every sprint of 8 or more working days: 96 reserve gates across 51 sprints, 10 percent of capacity. It is a calendar reserve, not a line item, so the dates above already include it. If a sprint ends with the reserve unspent, the next sprint starts its next story one day early; the schedule is re-issued at each milestone. Sprints 5 and 31 (the Christmas weeks, five working days each) have none. The cut-over's window does not spend it: the reserve days of sprints 1 to 6 stay held back, and the cut-over still ends 2027-01-13.

## Core's steady-state budget

Rule 17: after Core 1.0, a change between majors is additive, and a Core major ships with the line's move to the Unity LTS of its time. The budget is what one major costs, priced by analogy to the epics named, because no one has run a cycle. The developer's note on 2026-10-24 (answer 5) sets a major about every 12 months, an upgrade offered at a reduced cost and bug fixes with no API break for a year inside a major; Unity's LTS gap is about a year as the studio plans it, so the two coincide and the budget is per major. A major in which the runtime changes (Unity 7 and CoreCLR) adds the readiness work of UN8 once (5 gates); it is not in the per-cycle figure. Re-stated from the new tables: the release train row is WF5a's three stories, which now include the maintenance branch cut and protected in every repository, and the throwaway copy in the newest Unity alpha (U9 story 4) is a new row. The release scan joins the stored-format gate row, which stays one gate.

| Step | Priced like | Gates | Lines read | Lines written | Tokens (M) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Move to the new LTS | shape of MV | 4 | 1,100 | 250 | 0.56 |
| Ledger re-judged against the new LTS | shape of the UN1a check run | 1 | 700 | 200 | 0.16 |
| Canary editor and domain-reload run | shape of UN8 | 1 | 800 | 300 | 0.17 |
| Floor compile and test rows: the new row in, the row the floor leaves out | shape of LV7a | 2 | 600 | 300 | 0.29 |
| Core major: audit and surface review | shape of AUc | 3 | 3,000 | 500 | 0.51 |
| Stored-format gate over every package's surface file | shape of LV7d | 1 | 600 | 200 | 0.16 |
| Release order, one command, and the maintenance branch cut and protected in every repository | shape of WF5a, now 3 gates | 3 | 250 | 150 | 0.40 |
| Throwaway copy opened once in the newest Unity alpha | shape of U9 story 4 | 1 | 300 | 100 | 0.14 |
| Previous major kept for one cycle: backported fixes | my assumption, no epic to copy | 2 | 500 | 300 | 0.28 |
| Ledger re-judged at each Unity update release in the cycle (about three) | shape of the UN1a check run | 2 | 700 | 200 | 0.29 |
| Upstream drift: PurrNet, URP, the Steam SDK | my assumption, no epic to copy | 2 | 800 | 300 | 0.29 |
| Shipped games' saves checked against the new layouts | my assumption, shape of LV7d | 2 | 600 | 300 | 0.29 |
| Findings of the cycle's round fixed | shape of FX | 3 | 1,330 | 700 | 0.45 |
| One eight-lens round over the major | the milestone round | 0 |  |  | 0.91 |
| **Per major** |  | **27** |  |  | **4.89** |

**About 27 gates and 4.9 million tokens per major** (checkpoint 4: 25 and 4.6), with the floor compile and the test rows it carries. At two streams a year holds about 450 gates, so a Core major is about 6.0 percent of the capacity. Rule 17 binds only after Core 1.0 (estimated 2028-05-16), and the first Core major after it comes with the next Unity LTS, so none falls inside the plan; the first cycle's floor move is FL (2 gates, estimated 2027-12-13 to 2027-12-14). Not in the budget: the support routine (WF6, undated), the studio's own packages' minors and the backported fixes of the previous major beyond the two gates above.

## Sensitivity to the gate rate

The plan is capacity-bound, so the rate and the rules are the levers. Sprint 1 measures the rate (WF1a puts the fields and the report script in on day one). Each row is a fresh run of the scheduler; because an item that has started cannot be preempted, a small change of input can move a date by days in either direction. The last column is the day the cut-over's last part ends; the freeze starts 2027-01-14, so a date after it means the chat cut-over (UI4a) and its companions land after the festival. Every date is an estimate.

| Case | Saboteer on the line | Saboteer's first Steam release | Corsairs Isle on the line | End | Cut-over ends |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Plan: two streams, one gate per person per day, freeze from 14 January | 2028-01-06 | 2028-06-06 | 2028-04-19 | 2028-10-09 | 2027-01-13 |
| Freeze from the build date only (4 February) | 2028-01-06 | 2028-06-06 | 2028-04-19 | 2028-10-09 | 2027-01-13 |
| No freeze at all | 2028-01-06 | 2028-06-06 | 2028-04-19 | 2028-10-09 | 2027-01-13 |
| Saboteer's own code and scenes wait until after the festival, the cut-over included (manifest lines excepted) | 2028-01-06 | 2028-06-06 | 2028-04-19 | 2028-10-09 | 2027-04-19 |
| One editor per game at a time (the rule that was removed) | 2028-01-05 | 2028-07-17 | 2028-06-13 | 2028-10-09 | 2027-03-10 |
| Three streams (a third person at the same rate) | 2027-08-16 | 2027-11-24 | 2027-10-21 | 2028-02-24 | 2027-01-04 |
| One stream (half the rate, or one person) | 2029-02-27 | 2029-12-11 | 2029-09-26 | 2030-08-28 | 2027-04-15 |
| 0.50 gates per person per day | 2029-02-27 | 2029-12-28 | 2029-09-24 | 2030-09-04 | 2027-05-18 |
| 0.75 gates per person per day | 2028-06-27 | 2029-02-07 | 2028-11-22 | 2029-07-30 | 2027-04-07 |
| 0.93 gates per person per day | 2028-04-05 | 2028-10-12 | 2028-08-14 | 2029-03-22 | 2027-03-29 |
| 1.25 gates per person per day | 2027-11-09 | 2028-04-03 | 2028-02-17 | 2028-07-19 | 2027-01-08 |
| 1.50 gates per person per day | 2027-09-10 | 2028-01-13 | 2027-11-30 | 2028-04-13 | 2026-12-18 |
| Unity 6.7 ships after 2027-07-01 (only the move waits) | 2028-01-06 | 2028-06-06 | 2028-04-19 | 2028-10-09 | 2027-01-13 |
| One person away for the cut-over run-up (sprints 3 and 4), worse stream | 2028-01-18 | 2028-06-19 | 2028-05-02 | 2028-10-20 | 2027-03-16 |
| One person away for the festival run-up (sprints 7 and 8), worse stream | 2028-01-19 | 2028-06-20 | 2028-05-03 | 2028-10-23 | 2027-01-13 |
| One person away for sprint 10, worse stream | 2028-01-12 | 2028-06-13 | 2028-04-26 | 2028-10-16 | 2027-01-13 |
| One person away for the 2027 Christmas sprints (30 and 31), worse stream | 2028-01-17 | 2028-06-15 | 2028-05-01 | 2028-10-18 | 2027-01-13 |

Reading it: the freeze rows are equal because the work that would run in the freeze window is package work the streams carry anyway. The first tier changes no milestone: without it Saboteer is 2028-01-06 and Corsairs 2028-04-19, and the cut-over ends 2027-04-19, after the festival. The only rows that move the cut-over are the ones that take capacity before 14 January: at 0.93 it ends 2027-03-29 and one person away in sprints 3 and 4 moves it to 2027-03-16. A third person at the same rate brings Saboteer to 2027-08-16 and the cut-over to 2027-01-04. W4c in Saboteer's class (the answer the developer did not give) would put Saboteer at 2028-01-14, and as a Corsairs seed it would put Corsairs at 2028-05-02 and the first release at 2028-06-16. The first release has its own column: it follows the longest chain (124 gates, through Navigation's first epic and the racing and underwater strangers), so at 0.93 it is 2028-10-12, and if the release checklist did not wait for those strangers it would be 2028-05-19. Deferring 11 leaf parts (35 gates: A6, A7b, CL7, CL8, IN1, NV9, UI7b, UI7c1, UI7c2, UI8, WF8) ends the plan 2028-09-11, about 4 weeks sooner. Losing a quarter of the rate (1.00 to 0.75) costs about 31 weeks on Corsairs.

Smaller gates, each inside one epic, assumed at the area's recommendation: the front door's name (`<Name>Kit`), whether an agent may approve its own drafts (CL4, no unless one setting grants it), the release tool (WF4a, semantic-release with beta and release-candidate branches), the carrier for packed payloads (NW2, byte data on pooled buffers), whether Instancing may hold one culling routine of ours (UN5, yes, decided), and the far-field renderer (W6.1, none chosen).

## Calendar items outside the gate count

- **Steam's partner page:** the developer checks the festival dates, the last day a demo build may change and the registration steps before 14 December 2026. Third-party dates: Next Fest 22 February to 1 March 2027, press preview 11 February, registration about 10 January. The cut-over has no margin against a freeze that starts earlier than 2027-01-14.
- **Publisher enrolment:** five to seven business days; the developer's own story, undated, no gate.
- **Asset Store review:** the time between submitting and going live is not stated in any file and was not measured. It sits after the second milestone.
- **Unity 6.7 LTS:** expected at the end of 2026; `Unity.md` read 6000.7 as Beta on 2026-10-23. UN9 installs the beta in a throwaway copy of one dev project (2027-01-20 to 2027-01-26). MV does not start before 2027-01-04 and nothing waits for it. Unity 7 has no final date; UN9's fourth story opens one copy in the newest alpha once.
- **6.3 LTS end of support, December 2027:** the floor moves to 6.7 then (FL, 2 gates, estimated 2027-12-13 to 2027-12-14).
- **The merge of Modifiers' branch:** 2027-03-02 (estimated), after the festival's last gate; the developer's act.
- **Saboteer's first Steam release:** the act has no gate and the date is the developer's; LV6m and WF5m put the window's mode and the maintenance branch in place on the day it ships, estimated 2028-06-05 and 2028-06-06, after every Saboteer-tagged part, Corsairs' soak and the first stable tags. A release earlier than that is the question under Decisions.
- **Leave and public holidays:** priced as a rate row, not as dates.

## Red team: Roadmap and Time lenses, checkpoint 5

Lenses run: Roadmap and Time, the two the brief names for a roadmap. Skipped: the other five, because a roadmap's claims are order, placement and dates. Mode: self, Sonnet 5.5, author the same session. This session had no tool to start reader subagents, so each lens was a separate pass of mine, run the way the skill asks: write the strongest claim first, name the consumers, walk a task through the plan and the program's output, then try to kill the finding before recording it. The independent attack is the orchestrator's Opus readers afterwards. The strongest claim under Roadmap: no epic starts before what it needs, each milestone means what it says, and each answer of 2026-10-24 is in the plan as given. Under Time: the dates survive the freeze, a late Steam answer, a late Unity and a person away.

### Fixed

| Lens | Finding | What changed |
|---|---|---|
| Roadmap | Who: the developer, trying to see the new UI and chat in the demo. The first run put UI2 behind the festival: UI1 waited for the Studio.Tools shell (L11t) only for its Studio.Presentation story, and ran as one 11-gate part, so the chain L1, UI1, UI2, UI3, U4a was 43 gates against 41 usable days. Evidence: first run, UI2 start 2027-03-04 | UI1b is split off (that story waits for L11t); U4a is priced in three parts (library 5, view and settings page 5, cut-over 1) so the library runs beside the console; the closure is a first tier ordered by the longest chain inside it. The cut-over ends 2027-01-13 |
| Roadmap | Who: an agent building the cut-over. The check that no Saboteer rewire starts before CL5 and LV4a end refused the cut-over, which is the one rewire the developer put in front of the freeze | The cut-over parts are the named exception in the program; every other rewire still waits (L3 starts 2027-04-15) |
| Roadmap | Who: the developer reading "CL3's Saboteer half before the freeze". CL3 waited for WorldObjects (L3, R8a) as one part, so it could not land before the freeze | CL3a (2 gates, Saboteer's ten classes, waiting for UI3) and CL3b (4 gates, Corsairs' commands and the removal of the old types, waiting for L3 and R8a) |
| Roadmap | Who: the Unity owner, whose probe "waits for nothing and nothing waits for it". WF7b listed MV and UN9, which pulled both into Corsairs' class and made 6.7 a dependency | Both are off WF7b's list; the C++ runtime files choice is MV's, found by U9, and recorded in the release |
| Roadmap | Who: Networking's owner and World's owner, who each counted the far layer planning story as the other's. The plan carried NW8 as a priced item beside W6's 13 | NW8 is gone as an item; W6n is 1 of W6's 13 gates (W6b is 5), NW9 and W11 wait for it. Networking is 60 stories as filed |
| Roadmap | Who: ArtPipeline's A2, waiting for `IsPlayable`, which sat in R4 behind R3a, L7a and W2a. Evidence: R4's dependencies | R4p is R4's first story and needs only Character; A2 waits for it |
| Roadmap | Who: an artist, told that Send exists before the freeze. A5a needs WF1b, WF2b and L11t; none fit in the window, and A5a puts its caller into Saboteer's repository | A5a and WF1r are Saboteer-tagged and land 2027-03-12 to 2027-03-17 and 2027-03-18; the text no longer says before the freeze |
| Roadmap | Who: Workflow's WF5m and Living's LV6m, placed right after R7b so that no later Saboteer-tagged part comes before them. The independent readers showed the order was backwards: UN6, R2 and WF7b remove or replace surface, and a shipped Saboteer follows a branch that refuses removals | Superseded: the pair is the release itself and waits for every Saboteer-tagged part, Corsairs' soak and the first stable tags (Independent readers, findings 1, 6, 7, 8); the program refuses a Saboteer-tagged part that ends after the pair starts |
| Time | Who: anyone reading the paired-merge rule across Christmas. The check counted calendar days, so WF5m, two working days after R7b, was refused | The gap is counted in working days |
| Time | Who: the developer, trusting "a gate is the calendar". The cut-over fills both streams from 2026-11-02 and the reserve days of sprints 1 to 6 are held back; at 0.93 the cut-over ends 2027-03-29 | Not fixable by order (below); the sensitivity table has a column for it, and the demo section says what a slip costs: the chat cut-over, not the console |
| Time | Who: the developer, reading a milestone date that includes two gates a holiday apart. | Superseded: the pair left the milestone. Saboteer on the line is estimated 2028-01-06, its soak ends 2027-12-21, the first release is estimated 2028-06-06, and all three are printed |

### Examined and kept

- **UI7a and LV2's window shell sit on the spine to the first rewire** (L1 > CL1 > CL4 > UI7a > LV2 > LV4a > L3). Tooling on the way to the agents' context looked like a later improvement on the critical path. I ran the plan with UI7a off LV2 and with LV2 off LV4a: neither milestone moves, only the foundation waypoint, by up to three weeks, because capacity decides the end. Kept; a claim to Living that the runner could land before the window shell.
- **A7a, R8a and R8b add 16 days to Saboteer's milestone** (2027-12-21 to 2028-01-06), for nine gates that fall across the Christmas break. They put an artist's way from a model to a playable prop inside Saboteer's milestone (reader finding 5). Kept; the run without them is in the answer to that finding.
- **W4c behind the milestone and in no class.** The run with W4c as a Corsairs seed puts Corsairs 13 days later (2028-05-02) and the first release 10 days later; the run with W4c in Saboteer's class puts Saboteer at 2028-01-14.

### Could not fix

- **The cut-over has no margin.** It ends 2027-01-13, the working day before the freeze. The only spare capacity before the freeze is the reserve, four days per stream (2026-11-13, 2026-11-27, 2026-12-11, 2026-12-23); spending it would buy eight gates of margin and is the 10 percent the plan keeps for exactly this. A freeze that starts a week earlier than 14 January removes the last three parts (UI6b, UI4a and the second gate of CL3a) from the demo. The developer's answer 23 stands; the plan cannot do better without a third stream (the sensitivity table: 2027-01-04).
- **The cut-over is a Saboteer rewire before the agents' context.** CL5 ends 2027-02-02 and LV4a 2027-03-23, after the cut-over. The reader finding of checkpoint 4 (the context before every rewire) holds for every other rewire and is relaxed for these parts only.
- **The window pushes three things behind the festival that the owners asked to have in front of it.** The chat bridge U4b (Networking and UI asked for it in the demo), the minimal Send A5a (Art) and the demo's tag script WF7a (Workflow). The demo carries Saboteer's thin ChatNetwork and its chat corruption; the tag is made by hand.
- **The owners' classes differ from the plan's** in the places the table under "How the epics were assigned" lists; each goes back as a claim.
- **My splits and sizings** (CL1b, CL3a and CL3b, CL4b, UI1b, U4a's 5, 5 and 1, L8a's 2, R0d, A7a and A7b, R4p, W2c, W10aL, W6n, WF1r, WF5m, NW2g, and the earlier L1r, LV4a, A5a, L8c, L8u and MV) are mine; the owners receive each as a claim. Which 2 of L8's 6 gates are the slice is the Persistence pitch's to say.
- **The list scheduler is a heuristic.** The first tier and the boosts are my judgement and each moves other dates by days; the sensitivity table is a set of runs, not a prediction of the next one.
- **The gate rate and the token rates** are the decided and the checkpoint 2b ones, unmeasured until sprint 1.
- **Steam's own rules** are in no file I read, so the build date and the freeze are assumptions until the partner page is checked.
- **The independent readers had not run when this section was written;** their round is answered in the next section.

Could not check: whether each area's story counts are right (I priced the files as filed), whether Unity 6.7 LTS ships at the end of 2026, Steam's festival steps, and PurrNet or Unity behaviour (nothing here was run).

## Red team: independent readers, checkpoint 5

Opus readers, one lens each, attacked the hand-off and the folded areas; twelve findings landed on this file, five of them blockers or serious on one fault. Each is answered in plain words: fixed (the plan or the file changed), fixed in part, rejected with the reason, or could not fix. I ran the program again after every change; it reports no violations (198 parts). The one number I could not take from a reader is finding 12's "about 7 working days": the run says 13 days.

### The one fault behind findings 1, 2, 6, 7 and 8

The plan put Saboteer's first Steam release (LV6m and WF5m) right after Saboteer's own soak, in January 2028. From that day Saboteer follows `release-<major>`, and a pull request into that branch fails on any removed or changed public line. But three things the plan still had to do to Saboteer after that date remove surface: Core's ECS and its Entities line (UN4b), GeoForge's blob tables (W2c), and Saboteer's own move off them (UN6); Diagnostics (R2) and Studio.Online (L11b) were new repositories that did not exist when the branch was cut; and rule 15 makes a package 1.0 the day it ships to a customer while the final-tag chain (WF4b, LV7b, LV7c, Core 1.0) was dated after, or not at all. A builder could only break the guard, move Saboteer back to main against rule 17, or stop.

What I changed. LV6m and WF5m are the release itself now, and they wait for every Saboteer-tagged part (the program adds the dependency from the tags and refuses the plan if one ends after they start), for Corsairs' soak (R7c, which already waits for UN6 and UN4b), for Diagnostics and Studio.Online (R2b, L11b, L11c), and for the first stable tags: Core's audit and Core 1.0 (AUc, CT), the tag gate and the release checklist (LV7b, LV7c) and the candidate tag that starts the strangers run (WF5b). Those seven gates were undated; they are dated now. UN6, WF7b and R2a no longer wait for the pair. The release is estimated **2028-06-06** (2028-10-12 at 0.93), instead of 2028-01-03. Saboteer on the line stays a milestone (estimated 2028-01-06): the same game, on the same packages, still following the branches. Corsairs on the line is 2028-04-19. The release date is the developer's act and an estimate; the question of going out earlier is under Decisions, with the plan already built on the recommended answer.

### The twelve findings

| # | Lens and severity | Verdict | Answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Agent, blocker: Saboteer is on its maintenance branch before the breaking work that still has to reach it | Fixed | The release now follows UN4b, W2c, UN6, R2 and WF7b (the fault above). The suggested fix was either to date the release after UN6 and R7c or to move the removals in front of it; the removals depend on Corsairs' Instancing seam, so the release moves. The release date moved from 2028-01-03 to 2028-06-06 |
| 2 | Agent, serious: the first Steam release makes its packages ship before stable releases can exist | Fixed | WF4b (final tag mechanism, now 2028-05-02 to 2028-05-09), LV7b and LV7c (2028-05-11 to 2028-05-15 and 2028-06-02), AUc and CT (2028-05-08 to 2028-05-16) and WF5b are dated and the release waits for them. The beta-tag fallback in `Workflow.md` is not used by this plan; it is the January alternative under Decisions |
| 3 | Agent, minor: step 8 does not wait for the console and the chat it delivers | Fixed | LM and LS wait for UI4a, UI6b and CL3a, so Core's assemblies, the references, the console and the chat reach the followed branches on one day, 2027-01-13, the working day before the freeze. UI4a ends 2027-01-12. The hand-off's step 8 must wait for the steps that hold those three (below) |
| 4 | Agent, minor: UN9's copy uses a script built eight months later | Fixed | UN9's first copy is a plain folder copy that skips what Unity regenerates (Library, Temp, Logs); `run-local-tests.sh` is WF3a's and reuses the same exclusions later. No date moves. The held table in the line text and UN9's sentence in the hand-off must say it (below) |

A7a (4 gates: the variant, the preset and definition drafts, Approve) waits for A1b, R8b and CL4 and is Saboteer's class, with R8a and R8b, which it calls and which `Runtime.md` already classed as Saboteer's. A7b (1 gate: Open in gym) alone waits for W4c. Cost: Saboteer on the line moves from 2027-12-21 to 2028-01-06, because nine gates fall across Christmas |

The Corsairs milestone row now says Saboteer sheds Entities and repeats its soak before it ships |

The program now checks it |

R0 is nine gates and runs 2027-02-25 to 2027-03-11. The cut-over still ends 2027-01-13. The hand-off's R0 or L2c paragraph must name `Describe` |

Step 40's stop column still names the manual scan as a belt for the first upload |

Corsairs on the line is 13 days sooner than with W4c as a seed (2028-04-19 against 2028-05-02), not the 7 days the reader estimated. A7b, CL8 and W9b still wait for it |

### Could not fix, and why

- **The release date is the developer's.** I dated the act after the removals and the stable tags because the rules decided on 2026-10-24 (a shipped game follows the maintenance branch, a package is 1.0 the day it ships, a major about every 12 months) leave no other order that works. If the developer wants Saboteer on Steam in January 2028, the plan and the line text change (the question under Decisions).
- **The release waits for Navigation's first epic.** Living's release checklist (LV7c) waits for the racing and underwater strangers (LV5c, LV5d), which wait for NV2a. If the checklist did not wait for them the release would be 2028-05-19, 18 days sooner. A claim to Living; I did not change their dependency.
- **The audit pass of the other packages (AU, 2028-09-27 to 2028-09-28) and its fixes (FX5) come after the release.** If the audit finds a public line to remove or change in a package Saboteer installs, that change waits for the next major. Core's audit is before 1.0 (AUc); whether the others must be too is Living's and Workflow's to say, a claim to both.
- **R0d needs only Core's present packing.** I read `Describe` as a function over the types packing already registers; if it needs the packing slice (PK), L8a waits for PK and the cut-over loses its margin. Nothing here was run.
- **The hand-off files are not mine.** The sentences they carry that change are returned to the orchestrator in the result.

## Decisions

One. The plan is built on the recommended answer; the developer's other answer changes the dates and two sentences of the line text.

**Should Saboteer go on sale on Steam in June 2028, after the big removals and the first final package versions, and not in January 2028?**

- **Recommended: yes, June 2028.** Example: until 2028-06-06 the Spellbound window keeps Saboteer on the branches, so Core's ECS, GeoForge's blob tables and Saboteer's Entities can all be removed; on the release day the window switches Saboteer to `release-2` and every package it installs has a final tag.
- **Alternative: no, January 2028, on beta tags.** Example: Saboteer follows `release-2` from January; deleting Core's ECS and taking Entities out of Saboteer's manifest then wait for the next Core major, about 12 months later, the line text gains a rule for a game that ships before the final tags, and the Corsairs milestone stops saying Saboteer sheds Entities.

Nothing else waits on an answer. Where the plan and an answer pull against each other (the cut-over's missing margin, the context rule relaxed for the cut-over), the answer is applied and the cost is printed above, not asked again.

## Record: the checkpoint 4 result (2026-10-24)

Kept as the dated record. Its dates, its decisions (answered the same day: 1 yes, 2 no, 3 yes) and its scheduler belong to the checkpoint 4 plan, and the file above replaces them.

### The result in six lines, checkpoint 4

- **847 dated gates in 115 epics**, 140.9 million tokens, 159.9 with the red-team rounds (15.4 million for 48 epics of eight stories or more, 3.6 million for four milestone rounds). Outside the date: 21 undated gates (3.1 million) and 26 backlog gates (4.1 million) priced and not scheduled.
- **Two milestones, both measured by the games.** Saboteer on the line, estimated **2028-01-04** (sprint 31, 530 gates finished); Corsairs Isle on the line, estimated **2028-04-10** (sprint 38, 656 gates finished); the plan ends, estimated, 2028-09-08 (sprint 49). With leave and public holidays priced in (0.93 gates per person per day) the estimates are 2028-03-23, 2028-07-20 and 2029-02-01. The checkpoint 3 plan said 2028-02-24, 2028-06-21 and 2028-08-24. The readers' fixes of this checkpoint cost 8 weeks on Saboteer (decision 3).
- **Capacity decides the dates.** The longest chain is 100 gates to Saboteer's soak and 111 to Corsairs'; with unlimited streams and no freeze Saboteer would be on the line 2027-04-14 and Corsairs 2027-04-29 (the agents' context, CL5 and LV4a, now sits on that chain). The rules that went (one editor per game, the game days) were the lever: with the old one-editor rule on today's counts Saboteer is 2028-02-14 and Corsairs 2028-06-30, about 6 weeks and 12 weeks later.
- **Saboteer's demo is due before 11 February 2027 and the plan meets it, with one exposure.** The demo build's files are ready, estimated 2026-12-14, and no part touches Saboteer's code or scenes before 2027-03-11 (MV; the first rewire is L3, 2027-03-15), because the freeze runs from 14 January to 1 March. Nothing is pinned, so the packages Saboteer follows keep changing under the demo; decision 1 asks about the one change that can break its build. The freeze costs no days on either milestone, and none on the first rewire: L3 starts 2027-03-15 with or without it, because CL5 and LV4a must land first.
- **Navigation (46 gates after its spike) runs after the Corsairs milestone and before any launch**, estimated 2028-04-12 to 2028-08-16; the scheduler's check refuses any earlier start. Its spike, NV1, now runs before GeoForge's run-list read is built, estimated 2027-02-24 to 2027-03-02. Corsairs' launch has no date.
- Three decisions are left for the developer, listed last. Nothing else waits on an answer. The scheduler is `Workflow/roadmap_svg.py`; it checked 174 parts against their dependencies, the freeze, the Corsairs milestone gate, the shipped agent context before any rewire and the paired merges, with no violations.

### Sensitivity to the gate rate, checkpoint 4

The plan is capacity-bound, so the rate and the rules are the levers. Sprint 1 measures the rate (WF1a puts the fields and the report script in on day one). Each row is a fresh run of the scheduler; because an item that has started cannot be preempted, a small change of input can move a date by days in either direction. Every date is an estimate.

| Case | Saboteer on the line | Corsairs Isle on the line | End |
|---|---|---|---|
| Plan: two streams, one gate per person per day, freeze from 14 January | 2028-01-04 | 2028-04-10 | 2028-09-08 |
| Freeze from the build date only (4 February) | 2028-01-04 | 2028-04-10 | 2028-09-08 |
| No freeze at all | 2028-01-04 | 2028-04-10 | 2028-09-08 |
| Saboteer's own code and scenes wait until after the festival (manifest lines excepted) | 2028-01-04 | 2028-04-10 | 2028-09-08 |
| One editor per game at a time (the rule that was removed) | 2028-02-14 | 2028-06-30 | 2028-09-08 |
| Three streams (a third person at the same rate) | 2027-08-17 | 2027-10-14 | 2028-02-07 |
| One stream (half the rate, or one person) | 2029-02-13 | 2029-09-04 | 2030-07-02 |
| 0.50 gates per person per day | 2029-02-23 | 2029-09-05 | 2030-07-08 |
| 0.75 gates per person per day | 2028-06-16 | 2028-11-01 | 2029-06-11 |
| 0.93 gates per person per day | 2028-03-23 | 2028-07-20 | 2029-02-01 |
| 1.25 gates per person per day | 2027-11-03 | 2028-02-07 | 2028-06-21 |
| 1.50 gates per person per day | 2027-09-06 | 2027-11-17 | 2028-03-20 |
| Unity 6.7 ships after 2027-07-01 (only the move waits) | 2028-01-04 | 2028-04-10 | 2028-09-08 |
| One person away for the festival run-up (sprints 7 and 8), worse stream | 2028-01-18 | 2028-04-24 | 2028-09-22 |
| One person away for sprint 10, worse stream | 2028-01-10 | 2028-04-18 | 2028-09-18 |
| One person away for the 2027 Christmas sprints (30 and 31), worse stream | 2028-01-24 | 2028-04-20 | 2028-09-20 |

Reading it: the freeze rows are equal because the work that would run in the freeze window is package work the streams carry anyway; they do not even differ in when Saboteer's first rewire starts (L3 on 2027-03-15 either way), because the shipped agent context has to land first. The rule that was removed was worth about 6 weeks on Saboteer and 12 on Corsairs, less than at the last run because the plan is now held by the longer chain through the runner and the agents' context. A third person at the same rate brings Saboteer to 2027-08-17 and ends the plan 2028-02-07. One person away for a sprint or two costs up to three weeks at the milestones. A late Unity 6.7 moves nothing but MV and FL. Deferring 12 leaf epics (41 gates: A6, A7, CL7, CL8, IN1, LV5d, NV9, UI7b, the inspectors, UI8, WF8) ends the plan 2028-08-09, about 4 weeks sooner, and moves the milestones by days. Losing a quarter of the rate (1.00 to 0.75) costs about 29 weeks on Corsairs.

### Red team: Roadmap and Time lenses, checkpoint 4

Lenses run: Roadmap and Time, the two the brief names for a roadmap. Skipped: the other five, because a roadmap's claims are order, placement and dates. Mode: self, Sonnet 5.5, author the same session. I could not start separate reader sessions: this session has no agent tool, so each lens was a separate pass of mine, run the way the skill asks: write the strongest claim first, name the consumers, walk a task through the plan and the program's output, then try to kill the finding before recording it. The independent attack is the orchestrator's Opus readers afterwards, and they do not share my blind spots. The strongest claim under Roadmap: no epic starts before what it needs, and each milestone means what it says, with nothing waiting on a pin, the one-editor rule or Unity 6.7. Under Time: the dates survive the freeze, a late Steam answer, a late Unity and a person away. The program's check of every start against its dependencies held throughout; the findings are about what that check does not hold.

#### Fixed

| Roadmap | Runtime's world-step order test would have pulled all eight stories of NW3a into Saboteer's class | NW3s, the one Simulate story, is split off; R3d waits for it alone |

| Time | The manifest edits (LM, LS) waited for LV1b, which the list scheduler had placed after CL4; Core's restructure would have broken Saboteer's manifest line on 2026-11-20 and the edit would have come eight weeks later | LV1b is boosted so the layout lands with L1; LS runs on 2026-11-24 and the restructure merges only with it |

#### Could not fix

- **The packages Saboteer follows keep changing inside the freeze.** The developer decided that nothing is pinned. CL4's Modifiers story lands inside it (estimated 2027-01-18 to 2027-02-02); PK no longer does. Decision 1.
- **W4c, the Places gym, sets Saboteer's date.** Moved behind the milestone, Saboteer would be on the line 2027-12-08 and Corsairs 2028-03-29. The World owner's table puts W4c in Saboteer's class and I kept it. Decision 2.
- **Saboteer's soak waits for tooling.** R7b waits for the local gate, the push gate and the release order (WF3a, LV7a, WF5a) as the safety net for its own runs. Dropping those three waits would bring Saboteer to 2027-12-21, 2 weeks sooner, so I kept them.
- **The list scheduler is a heuristic.** The boosts are my judgement and each moves other dates by days; the sensitivity table is a set of runs, not a prediction of the next one.
- **Overlaps counted as filed.** The Entities removal in W1, W2 and UN6, GeoForge.Networking in NW2 and W2b, and the floor compile in WF3 and LV7 may be counted twice, up to about 10 gates.
- **My splits and sizings** (R1b, R2, R3, R6, R7a to R7c, R8, the parts of W1, W2, W6, W7, W9, UN1, UN4, LV1, LV3, LV5, WF1 to WF5, CL6, NW3, L3's collision story, L11's house set, the restructure stories, NW8 and, from the readers, L1r, LV4a, LV4b, A5a, A5b, L8c, L8u and MV's three added stories) are mine; the owners receive each as a claim. Which Networking story is the Simulate story is Networking's to say.
- **The gate rate and the token rates** are the decided and the checkpoint 2b ones, unmeasured until sprint 1.
- **Steam's own rules** are in no file I read, so the build date of 2027-02-04 is an assumption until the partner page is checked.
- **No independent reader had run on this file when this section was written.** The Opus readers ran afterwards; their findings and my answers are in the next section.

Could not check: whether each area's story counts are right (I priced the files as filed), whether Unity 6.7 LTS ships at the end of 2026, Steam's festival steps, and PurrNet or Unity behaviour (nothing here was run).

| 2 | A4 takes the hand-placed grip away months before the artist can see a held prop | Right, fixed | A4 waits for A1b (the verdict window, 2027-08-10 to 2027-08-19) and A3 (the hold test, 2027-09-22 to 2027-09-28); A4 runs 2027-11-04 to 2027-11-10, five weeks later than before. Both join Saboteer's class, 13 gates. Decision 2 did not name artists as the reader says; it does now |

A5a is the minimal Send, 4 gates, boosted, estimated 2026-12-04 to 2026-12-09: the folder-based send set, the commit subject the title rule reads and the push, with no window and no checks, which is how it fits before the freeze. A5b is the rest and the check at Send. The reader's other fix, to switch the artist ruleset on in a game repository only when A5a lands there, is a story inside Workflow's WF1, so it is a claim to Workflow; if WF1 stays as filed the gap is the two weeks after WF1b ends on 2026-11-26 |

L1r (3 gates, in L1, boosted) adds the references; LM and LS wait for it; L1 merges to a followed branch only with them and the program refuses a gap of more than a week. The demo section's "mostly additive" is rewritten. Not checked: each of the 68 files' assemblies one by one, which L1r's first story lists |

It is boosted: R6b ends 2027-11-11 and R6c runs 2027-11-15 to 2027-11-19. R6b merges to Inventory's followed branch only with R6c, and the program refuses a gap of more than a week. R7c still waits for R6c. A claim to Runtime |

R3a is 7 (a claim to Runtime). The first user, W1a, starts 2027-02-10. NW1, W4b and R3a now list L2c, so the program checks them |

The four gates are a placeholder, because Persistence has no area owner and its pitch (PP, 2027-07-14 to 2027-07-19) sizes it. The reader counts ten Corsairs store files; I count 29 files in Corsairs' game code that mention Persistence and did not sort the stores from the rest |

NV1 runs 2027-02-24 to 2027-03-02 and W2a starts 2027-04-13, so a no-go or a different run-list shape arrives before the read and the change event are built. NV1 joins Saboteer's class, 5 gates. NV1 measures GeoForge's run lists as they are today, as Pathfinding wrote it |
| 9 | Saboteer's first Steam release has no saves upgrade and no stored-format gate before late 2028 | Right, fixed in part | L8u (4 gates, a placeholder for the Persistence pitch) is the upgrade. LV7d (1) and the LV3b it needs (5) come into Saboteer's class, and R7b waits for L8u and LV7d, so Saboteer on the line now means it can ship with its players' saves intact. LV7d is estimated 2027-12-01 and L8u 2027-11-11 to 2027-11-17. The release itself is the developer's act and has no gate in the plan. The reader's other option, to say Saboteer does not ship before a date, is decision 3's alternative |
| 10 | MV is still priced at 4 gates after the 6.7 summary named its work | Right, fixed | MV is 7 gates: the four of the move and three named stories (the Localization migration in Saboteer's eight files, the saved-reference cycle rules in 11 Modifiers, 3 Core and 1 Corsairs files, and the SSAO settings with the C++ runtime files in the Steam upload). Lines read 1,800 and written 600 are my estimates from the summary's counts; nothing was run |

**What the fixes cost, and what I could not fix.** The order the readers ask for puts Saboteer on the line 2028-01-04 and Corsairs 2028-04-10, against 2027-11-10 and 2028-02-29 before: 8 and 6 weeks. Taken out one at a time, the agents' context, the artist's tools, the saves upgrade and the Navigation spike each cost 2 to 3 weeks; the first three together about 5 weeks. Decision 3 asks. The run-to-run noise of a list scheduler is about a week, so I trust the order and the gaps more than any one date. I could not fix: (a) when Workflow's artist ruleset switches on, which is Workflow's story; (b) the sizes of L8c and L8u, which wait for the Persistence pitch; (c) that A5a sends without a check, so the first sends are checked by the reviewer only until A5b; (d) the claims this round created to Art, Living, Runtime, Workflow and the Persistence pitch, which wait for their owners; (e) the reader's three extra agent tasks, which I did not walk. No fix changes the decided line or reopens a developer's answer: L8u is what the answer to question 2 asks for, and the A5 and LV4 splits are sizing.

## Record: the checkpoint 3 result (2026-10-23)

Kept as the dated record. Its dates, its pin gate (SFp), its one-editor rule and its game days belong to the checkpoint 3 plan, and the file above replaces them. Its seven decisions were all answered on 2026-10-24; the answers are in the table at the end of this section.

### The result in six lines, checkpoint 3

- **786 dated gates in 106 epics**, 131.9 million tokens, 150.0 million with the red-team rounds (14.4 million for 45 epics of eight stories or more, 3.6 million for four milestone-style rounds). Outside the date: 15 undated gates (2.2 million) after the second milestone, and 26 backlog gates (4.1 million) priced and not scheduled.
- **Two milestones, both measured by the games.** Saboteer on the line **2028-02-24** (sprint 35, 568 gates finished); Corsairs Isle on the line **2028-06-21** (sprint 43, 711 gates finished); the whole plan ends **2028-08-24** (sprint 48). With leave and public holidays priced in (0.93 gates per person per day) the dates are 2028-04-13, 2028-09-26 and 2029-01-10. Core 1.0, the first listing, enrolment and every store step have no date.
- **Capacity decides the date, and the one-editor-per-game rule and the game days decide the milestones.** The longest chain is 80 gates to Saboteer (91 to the audit); with unlimited streams, no game rule, no freeze and no game days Saboteer would land 2027-03-12 and Corsairs 2027-03-26. Two streams end 2028-08-24. If two people could work in the same game at once, Saboteer lands 2027-11-05, sixteen weeks sooner. One day in every sprint belongs to each game's own work (decision 6); without it Saboteer lands 2028-01-19, five weeks sooner.
- **Saboteer's demo is due before 11 February 2027 and the plan meets it on today's code.** SFp pins Saboteer's package lines to commits on 2026-11-02, before L1 and LV1b change those packages, and the demo build's files and tag script (WF7a) are ready 2026-11-19, seven weeks before registration opens (about 10 January). It cannot contain the line: Saboteer on the line comes a year after the festival. Details in "Saboteer's demo and Next Fest".
- Seven questions are left for the developer, listed last. Nothing else waits on an answer.
- The scheduler is `Workflow/roadmap_svg.py`, re-issued with this pass's epic data and the game-day rule (the orchestrator installs it). It holds the epic data, schedules, checks every start (157 parts against their dependencies, no violations, no two Saboteer-tagged or Corsairs-tagged parts at once, none of Saboteer's in the freeze, none of a game's tagged parts on its game day) and draws the chart.
